### Phase 5 — Model Training (LightGBM + Optuna + MLflow) *(3–4 days)*

**What this achieves:** A reproducible, experiment-tracked training pipeline that decides the model family, freezes the feature set, tunes hyperparameters, and logs the result as a run artifact. Five ordered steps — candidate comparison → decision → feature selection → Optuna tuning → model logging — each logged as its own MLflow run under experiment `telco-churn-training`. The phase deliberately stops short of the model *registry*: the artifact it produces is uncalibrated and therefore not a valid rollback target (`CLAUDE.md` § *Run artifacts vs. registry versions*); Phase 6 registers the calibrated pipeline. All decision logic lives in `src/`; the `03a`–`03c` notebooks import from it. **`03b`/`03c` render results computed elsewhere; `03a` is the exception — see the model-family retirement note below.**

**Order of operations (do not reorder — each step freezes an input to the next):**
1. **Build candidates** — `DummyClassifier(strategy='prior')`, `LogisticRegressionCV` (linear preprocessor: OHE `drop='first'` + `StandardScaler`), and default-config LightGBM (tree preprocessor), scored on one shared `RepeatedStratifiedKFold(5×3)` over the development split so folds are paired across candidates. `class_weight='balanced'` handles the ~27% positive rate for both families; a `DummyClassifier` leakage canary is a hard assertion (ROC-AUC ≈ 0.5, PR-AUC ≈ prevalence), not a soft warning. **Notebook-only as of the model-family retirement below — not part of the automated `python -m telco_churn.models.train` composition path.**
2. **Compare on PR-AUC** — a pre-registered paired-bootstrap decision rule (materiality Δ\*=0.005) decides LightGBM vs. LogReg (one-metric invariant: PR-AUC alone selects). Non-gating fixed-recall and per-segment fairness/robustness diagnostics are logged alongside but never decide the family. **Notebook-only, same as Step 1.**
3. **Generative diagnostic loop (2c/2d)** — human-in-the-loop bias/variance and OOF segment profiling on the development set only, between Steps 2 and 3; engineer a feature and re-enter at Step 2, or stop (≈3-round cap).
4. **Select features — retired as a live CV decision, kept as a diagnostic.** The 100-fold CV keep-vs-reduce wrapper (`run_selection_cv`) was retired after its own paired-bootstrap result was conclusive and consistent under repeat-count truncation: **full beats reduced** (Δ = 0.008 PR-AUC, CI [0.005, 0.011], p < 0.001), so `decide_survivors`'s `"reduced"` branch has never shipped and the committed feature set is now a frozen `FEATURE_SCHEMA` constant (all twenty columns, including the demographic block `[gender, seniorcitizen, has_partner, dependents]` as a `correlated_groups` joint-permutation entry). What's kept and still runs every cycle: one all-dev `mint_committed_list` fit for the permutation-importance table, the SHAP audit, and the correlated-group joint permutation — these feed the model card and `error_analysis.py`, never a promotion decision. Ablation recorded in `ANALYSIS.md` §4b; artifacts in MLflow run `de4fac8e`.
5. **Tune LightGBM with Optuna** — 50 TPE trials on PR-AUC after the freeze; `n_estimators` resolved per fold by early stopping, not searched (median `best_iteration_` across folds, each trial's early-stopping validation slice carved from that fold's own *training* partition so it never leaks into the CV score); a 1-SE selection rule over raw argmax picks the most-regularized trial within noise of the best.
6. **Log the tuned pipeline to the run — do not register it** — the final fit refits the selected trial on all of development, with **`n_estimators` scaled from the early-stopped median to this fit's larger row count** (`n_estimators_final = median(best_iteration_) × n_final_fit / n_fold_fit`, since the per-fold median was measured on a smaller carved-out training partition than the final fit actually trains on; a two-count CV diagnostic — scoring the tuned spec at both the raw and the scaled count on the same folds — confirms the correction against this project's own data rather than trusting the formula alone, and is logged alongside the full derivation in `training_manifest.json`). The full `[preprocessor → model]` `Pipeline` is then logged as pyfunc with `pyfunc_predict_fn="predict_proba"`, a signature inferred from that same probability output, and a log→reload→`predict_proba` parity check, retrievable at `runs:/<run_id>/model`; `training_manifest.json` records the engineering audit trail (git SHA, DVC hash, hyperparameters, feature space/columns, CV PR-AUC, paired-Δ vs. LogReg, and the returned `ModelInfo`'s **`logged_model_id`** — MLflow 3's `LoggedModel` id, which does not auto-populate on the registry and must be persisted deliberately or Phase 7's evaluation logging has no `model_id` to attach sealed-test metrics to). Uncalibrated and un-thresholded — not serving-ready, therefore **not a registry version**. See `CLAUDE.md` § *Run artifacts vs. registry versions*: the registry holds only valid rollback targets, and an uncalibrated pipeline is a stage of construction, not a deployable model. Phase 6 `calibrate.py` performs the single registration of this training cycle, on the calibrated artifact, and points `challenger` at it.

**Deliverables:**

*Configs:* `configs/training/lightgbm.yaml` (searched ranges + fixed determinism/imbalance knobs), `configs/training/logreg.yaml`, `configs/training/feature_selection.yaml`, `configs/tuning/optuna.yaml` (`n_trials`, sampler, pruner, `selection_rule`).

*Source:*
- `src/telco_churn/features/preprocessing.py` — adds `build_linear_preprocessor` alongside the existing tree-family `build_preprocessor`
- `src/telco_churn/features/schema.py` — adds a frozen `FeatureSchema` dataclass owning the binary/multi_cat/numeric column groups
- `src/telco_churn/data/split.py` — canonical dev/test split (sealed once, order-invariant), imported by every downstream consumer
- `src/telco_churn/models/train/` — one module per step (`candidates.py`, `comparison.py`, `feature_audit.py`, `tuning.py`, `log_model.py`) plus shared `common.py` (fold-parallel CV scoring, default hyperparameters, dev-split loading through `features/accessor.py::load_features()` — never an inline-constructed path — and tracking-URI/git/DVC resolution — `_resolve_tracking_uri` anchors a relative fallback to the project root and defaults a fresh clone or CI run with no `.env`/infra profile to `sqlite:///mlflow.db`, never the bare `mlruns` file store MLflow 3.14 has frozen and which raises on use); Optuna trials nested under a `tuning_study` parent run, its study name content-addressed on `data_content_hash` (a real `sha256` of the processed features, via `features_sha256()`) and the search-space config — a data change alone starts a fresh study rather than silently resuming one tuned against stale data. Every step-level run (`model_comparison`, `feature_selection`, `tuning_study`), not only the per-candidate ones, logs its input dataset via `common.py::_log_dev_input`, resolving through `features/accessor.py`'s canonical path rather than a hardcoded one, so `mlflow.log_input`'s lineage is legible on every run. `log_model.py`'s `run_model_logging_step` logs the pipeline and manifest onto the `tuning_study` run but passes no `registered_model_name=` and sets no alias — those two lines move to Phase 6 `calibrate.py`
- `src/telco_churn/features/select.py` — `mint_committed_list` (all-dev fit powering the permutation-importance table, SHAP audit, and correlated-group joint permutation); the CV keep-vs-reduce wrapper and its `"reduced"` branch are deleted, not retained dead — the record lives in `ANALYSIS.md` §4b and MLflow run `de4fac8e`, not in a live callsite
- `src/telco_churn/models/diagnostics.py` — pure helpers for the 2c/2d loop (`generalization_gap`, `learning_curve_points`, `segment_oof_errors`) and Step 2's fairness/robustness diagnostics
- `docker/mlflow/Dockerfile`, `sql/schema/000_create_mlflow_db.sql` — Postgres-backed MLflow tracking server

*Tests:* `tests/unit/test_train_*.py`, `test_select.py`, `test_diagnostics.py`, `test_split.py` — leakage-canary assertion, run-twice determinism, leak-free-refit proofs, synthetic planted-signal/noise fixtures; `tests/integration/test_train_subprocess.py` — full CLI composition path via Hydra fast-path config overrides.

*Notebooks:* `03a-model-selection.ipynb` (candidate comparison, bias/variance loop, fairness/robustness panel), `03b-feature-selection.ipynb` (selection experiment), `03c-hyperparameter-tuning.ipynb` (Optuna study, full→reduced→tuned progression, model-logging confirmation).

**Verification:** `uv run python -m telco_churn.models.train` completes 50 trials and logs a tuned pipeline at `runs:/<run_id>/model` with the reload-parity assertion passing and `training_manifest.json` attached; `telco-churn-pipeline` has **no new registry version** at the end of this phase (the registry stays empty until Phase 6 registers the calibrated artifact). LightGBM clears the Dummy floor and beats/ties LogReg under the decision rule; full `pytest` green. See `ANALYSIS.md` §4 for the recorded results and rationale, `CHANGELOG.md` `[0.5.0]`–`[0.5.3]` for delivery history, and `[0.6.2]`–`[0.6.3]` for the tracking-URI, dataset-lineage, and tree-count-scaling fixes applied to this phase's code after Phase 6 shipped.

> **⚠ Model-family retirement removes Steps 1-2 from `python -m telco_churn.models.train`'s composition path — mirrors the feature-selection retirement two steps later in this same phase.** `candidates.py::run_candidate_step` and `comparison.py::run_comparison_step` once ran on every automated invocation, re-deciding LightGBM-vs-LogReg from scratch each cycle — including every one of Phase 10's monthly retrains, the same comparability/cost problem the feature-selection freeze (Step 4 below) already solves for the adjacent axis. They are decoupled, not deleted: fully intact and tested in `src/`, called only from `03a-model-selection.ipynb`'s on-demand review, on a real trigger (a new candidate family, a drift signal, a scheduled periodic review), never automatically. The committed family is frozen into **`models/train/common.py::COMMITTED_MODEL_FAMILY`** (currently `"lightgbm"`, decided by MLflow run `01a0100b92a34c7a964879cf541fd6f3` — see `ANALYSIS.md` §4a's retirement note); `training_manifest.json`'s `model_comparison` section is replaced by a `model_family_committed` reference section (`CLAUDE.md`'s `training_manifest.json` spec is corrected accordingly). The Verification line's "50 trials... `training_manifest.json` attached" assertions are unaffected — nothing about Steps 3-5 changes; only Steps 1-2's call site moves.

---

### Phase 6 — Calibration + Cost-Sensitive Threshold *(2 days)*

**What this achieves:** The model outputs calibrated probabilities instead of raw, imbalance-reweighted scores, and the decision threshold reflects the actual business cost of a missed churner versus a wasted retention call — not the default 0.5. Three cost scenarios are shipped so the business can choose its own risk posture without re-deriving anything. `calibrate.py` fits and logs the calibrated pipeline but registers nothing — `models/register.py` (Phase 7) is the training cycle's single registration point, run as its own CLI step once `calibrate.py` has exited. **`threshold.py`'s last step is a pre-seal screen, not a footnote.** Before the sealed test set is ever touched, it re-runs `ANALYSIS.md` §0's V1/V2/V2b dev-OOF diagnostics, the aggregate calibration-slope band check, and V3 direction sanity — the model's single binding fairness-adjacent-shaped veto, computed here because it needs SHAP on the fitted model and this is the earliest point that exists — against the model's own dev-OOF probabilities, and hard-stops the cycle if either binding check fails. A check that can only reject, never approve, costs nothing to run here and everything to skip — this is the one point in the pipeline a badly-calibrated or artifact-fitting model can be caught without spending the one-time sealed-test evaluation (Phase 7) on it.

**Order of operations (do not reorder — each step depends on the last):**
1. **Calibrate** — `CalibratedClassifierCV(ensemble=False)` wraps the **unfitted** `[preprocessor → LightGBM]` `Pipeline`, resolved via `sklearn.clone(mlflow.sklearn.load_model(manifest["logged_model_uri"]))` — never `runs:/<run_id>/model`, which becomes ambiguous once this module logs a second model onto the same run. `ensemble=False` cross-fits on the development set (an explicit, seeded outer/inner `StratifiedKFold(5)`) with no static val holdout, and collapses `calibrated_classifiers_` to one entry whose `.estimator` is the Pipeline Phase 7's SHAP step needs. The resulting dev-OOF probability vector — the numbers that select the calibration method, produce BSS, validate `t*`, and (below) power the dev-OOF screen — is logged as a run artifact (`calibration/dev_oof_predictions.parquet`: `customer_id`, `y_true`, `p_hat`), not only summarized into JSON and discarded; the threshold step, Phase 7's drift reference, and `error_analysis.py` all read this vector directly rather than recomputing it (`CLAUDE.md` § *Persist the evidence, not just the conclusion*). A `calibration_spec` block (`method`, `inner_cv_folds`, `random_state`, `ensemble`) is written alongside in `calibration_summary.json`, so the calibration method and its exact configuration are frozen and legible without re-deriving them from `configs/` later.
2. **Select the calibration method** — sigmoid is the incumbent; isotonic must earn the switch. A hard PR-AUC-preservation gate runs first (`training_setup.delta_threshold`, per-fold mean AP, never pooled — pooling would dilute isotonic's ranking damage across five distinct calibration maps). Only a method that clears the gate proceeds to a paired-bootstrap CI on per-fold Brier (block-bootstrapped by outer fold, reusing `models/train/comparison.py`'s paired-bootstrap idiom). Both methods and their Brier/ECE/PR-AUC are logged regardless of which wins, so the loser's numbers make the winner's selection legible. The selected method's dev-OOF **calibration slope** (`calibrate.py::calibration_slope` — regress `y` on `logit(p)`, Cox calibration, with a bootstrap CI) is reported in `calibration_summary.json` beside Brier/BSS/ECE: it is a calibration diagnostic computed where the calibration decision is made, and it is what step 7 below screens against the band, and what §0's promotion guardrail later consumes on the sealed-test side at Phase 7.
3. **Log — fit and log the calibrated pipeline; register nothing.** `calibrate.py` calls `mlflow.sklearn.log_model(..., name="calibrated_model")` onto Phase 5's run — no `registered_model_name=`, no alias, no registry write of any kind. `name="calibrated_model"` (not `"model"`) is load-bearing — reusing the name would rebind `runs:/<run_id>/model` away from Phase 5's pipeline. Blocked outright (`RuntimeError`) if `training_manifest.json`'s `tuning_summary.trial_count_below_threshold` is true, overridable only by an explicit config flag (`calibration.override_trial_count_gate`) — a data-quality gate on the tuning result, not a performance comparison. `calibrate.py` writes `reports/calibrate_receipt.json` (`{run_id}`) — the pointer every downstream stage (`threshold.py`, `evaluate.py`, `error_analysis.py`, and `register.py`'s mint step) bootstraps its model reference from when no explicit override is passed. **Registration is `register.py`'s alone** (Phase 7, run as its own CLI step after `calibrate.py` has exited): `register_challenger` mints the version from `calibrate_receipt.json` (or an explicit `register.run_id=`), tags it `promotion_status: pending` at mint — before `threshold.py`'s dev-OOF screen, `evaluate.py`, or `error_analysis.py` can fail, so a crash anywhere downstream leaves the version in the one state every abort path is guaranteed to have started from — and points `challenger` at it. The version also carries a **`logged_model_id`** tag (from `ModelInfo.model_id`, since `ModelVersion.model_id` does not auto-populate in OSS MLflow) and a **`training_data_scope`** tag.
   - **Also freezes the golden-parity fixture, at this exact moment, inside `calibrate.py`.** `select_golden_rows(X_dev, customer_ids, n_rows)` picks the `cfg.calibration.golden_n_rows` (5) dev rows with the **lowest `customerid`** — never a positional `.head()`, so the fixture is stable against an unrelated reordering of the processed feature table upstream — and scores them with the **in-memory** fitted pipeline before `log_model`/pickling touches it at all. Logged as `calibration/golden_predictions.json`: the pinned `customerid`s, the rows themselves (at the model's committed input schema), the reference `p_hat` scores, and a `"purpose": "serving-parity fixture — reproducibility only; scores are in-sample and are not performance evidence"` key (every golden row is a development-partition row this model trained on). This is what makes `register.py`'s serving-parity smoke check a genuine independent round trip rather than a tautology — the reference must come from a different process and a different code path than the assertion that later checks it, and it is captured here, before this run's own model has ever been reloaded from disk.
   - **Also computes and logs the dev-side SHAP surface `threshold.py`'s V3 screen (step 7) judges.** For every transformed feature: `(mean_abs_shap, direction)`, plus the full `dev_shap_values.parquet` — `calibrate.py` holds the fitted pipeline in memory, so this is the only place a model/SHAP-matrix version mismatch structurally cannot occur.
4. **Derive the threshold** — closed form `t* = c / (r × LTV)`, from `q·r·LTV − c > 0` (contact iff expected value exceeds doing nothing). This supersedes the classical `C_FP/(C_FP + C_FN)` cost-matrix rule, which implicitly treats correct decisions as free; here every contact costs `c` regardless of outcome. `c`, `LTV`, and `r` are resolved per scenario from `configs/costs.yaml` (ARPU from churner `MonthlyCharges` quantiles, development set only; `r` — the one parameter this dataset cannot supply — taken from an industry benchmark range, not fit).
5. **Validate, don't select, against the data** — an empirical argmax-EV check (`cross_val_predict` over the calibrated pipeline) confirms `t*` falls inside a 1,000-resample bootstrap CI of where realized expected value actually peaks. `t*` itself has zero sampling variance (a closed-form function of cost parameters alone), so nothing here re-derives it — the CI describes the noisy empirical estimate, not the threshold. A retention-rate sensitivity sweep (holding cost/LTV fixed) is logged as a headline result, not a footnote: `t*` is inversely proportional to `r`, the model's single largest source of uncertainty.
6. **Ship — and split the output along the line the closed form already draws.** Every scalar this phase computes — `dev_brier`, `dev_bss`, `dev_ece`, `dev_per_fold_mean_ap`, `dev_calibration_slope` (step 2), and per scenario `t_star_{scenario}`, `implied_contact_rate_{scenario}`, `dev_ev_at_t_star_{scenario}` — is logged as an MLflow **metric**, not only as a field inside a JSON blob; the EV curve itself is persisted as `threshold/ev_curve.parquet` rather than only rendered. JSON remains the audit record. `threshold.py::expected_value_at_threshold(proba, y, scenario, t)` is the one place `p·r·LTV − c` is computed as a pure function; Phase 7's `economics.py` imports it rather than redefining it. `threshold/threshold.json` (all three scenarios' full diagnostic bundles) is logged to the run, alongside `threshold/figures/`. What it mirrors out to disk splits in two, because the file mixes two kinds of content with two different lifetimes:
   - **`configs/policy/threshold.yaml` — the policy, and *nothing model-dependent*.** All of it is a pure function of `configs/costs.yaml`, because `t* = c/(r × LTV)` **is model-independent by construction**. Provenance here is pinned by a **`costs_config_hash`**, not by `model_run_id`/`model_version` — same mechanism `CLAUDE.md` already mandates for the economics metrics.
   - **`threshold/threshold_validation.json` — the model-dependent half, logged as an artifact on the model's run.** `model_run_id`, `model_version`, `calibration_method`, `argmax_ev_threshold`, `argmax_ev_bootstrap_ci`, `within_ci`, `implied_contact_rate`. **Same rule as `drift_reference.json`: an artifact describing a specific version travels with that version, never sits at a fixed path.**
   - **⚠ Why the split, and not a restamp.** The model stamp is honest *today*. It goes wrong once a future retrain promotes a new challenger over the incumbent: the stamp does not change, the champion does, and `implied_contact_rate` silently becomes the *wrong model's* contact rate. Rewriting the stamp on promotion would be worse — it would convert a *visibly stale* stamp into a *silent misattribution*. Splitting removes the stamp from the file that has no business carrying one.
   - **Retires a third copy of the calibration method** — `calibration_method` now lives only in `calibration_summary.json` (source of truth) and `threshold_validation.json` (a record of what was calibrated), not a third time in `threshold.yaml`.
7. **Screen — the pre-seal dev-OOF check, and this module's own last step.** Immediately after deriving and shipping the threshold above, `run_threshold_step` re-uses the exact aligned `(customerid, y_true, p_hat)` vector it already built for the derivation — never a second fetch of `dev_oof_predictions.parquet` — and joins it to the `dev` partition's raw segment/protected columns (`build_dev_oof_screen_frame`, reading the full feature table via `features/accessor.py::load_features()` + `data.split.partition()`, restricted to `dev` only: this is the one place in `threshold.py` that imports `telco_churn.data.split`, and it never touches `test`).
   - **Two binding criteria, collected before raising, not two raises.** `screen_passed` is `calibration_slope ∧ v3_direction_sanity` — a `failures: list[{criterion, detail, remediation}]` accumulates both checks before `run_threshold_step` raises, so a simultaneous failure on both is never masked by whichever check happens to run first.
   - **The aggregate calibration-slope screen.** `calibrate.py`'s already-logged, already-selected slope (from `calibration_summary.json`, never recomputed) is checked against §0's `[0.80, 1.25]` band via `gate.py::slope_passes` — promoted from a private `_slope_passes` to a public function precisely so this module reuses the identical check `gate.py`'s own cold-start/comparative decisions apply, rather than reimplementing the band logic a second time. Measured at n = 5,634 (higher-powered than the eventual sealed-test slope's n = 1,409, but mildly optimistic, since Phase 6 selected the calibration method on these same probabilities).
   - **V3 — direction sanity, relocated here from a post-seal, non-binding check in Phase 7.** `explain.py::direction_sanity_check` ranks `calibrate.py`'s dev-side `(mean_abs_shap, direction)` summary to its own `v3_top_k_features`, then verdicts each against `EXPECTED_EDA_DIRECTIONS`, below a `v3_min_direction_magnitude` floor (weak relationships record `weak=True` rather than a violation) — both constants dev-derived, not inherited from the old test-side `8`. Zero features checked is itself a screen failure, not a vacuous pass. Binding because a sign-flipped top-feature relationship passes all four §0 gate criteria by construction — no aggregate metric reveals it — and a human reviewer with `require_review: false` (Phase 10) is nobody at all.
   - **Both are screens, not the gate — they can reject early, never approve.** **On failure, `run_threshold_step` raises `RuntimeError` after logging**, naming every failed criterion with its own numbers and remediation: the model never reaches `evaluate.py`, the sealed test set stays unspent, and the version sits at `promotion_status: pending` (never `rejected` — no evaluation verdict was ever reached) until it is re-calibrated and the cycle re-run. `register.py` re-checks both independently as a backstop for the out-of-band `evaluate` → `register` path.
   - **V1/V2/V2b — computed here, not in Phase 7.** `compute_dev_oof_diagnostics` calls `diagnostics.py`'s shared slicing helpers — `sliced_ranking_metrics`/`flag_segment_collapse` (V1, segment collapse), `sliced_decision_rates` + `equal_opportunity_difference_by_axis`/`demographic_parity_difference_by_axis` (V2, fairness disparity at the decision), `sliced_calibration`/`flag_calibration_collapse` (V2b, per-group calibration collapse) — on this dev-OOF frame. These are the same functions Phase 7's `evaluate.py` calls on its own sealed-test frame for its reporting-only slices, so the two surfaces (dev-OOF, screened; sealed-test, reported) are never two implementations of one idea. Reported, never gating (`ANALYSIS.md` §0): a flagged segment or fairness gap changes nothing about the promotion decision — it only feeds the model card and ongoing monitoring.
   - **Writes and logs `reports/dev_oof_predictions.parquet` + `reports/dev_oof_diagnostics.json`** (the latter mirrored under the run's `threshold/` artifact folder), plus `dev_oof_calibration_slope` (+ CI bounds) as metrics against the model's `model_id` and a named `dev_oof_screen` MLflow dataset, and a `dev_oof_screen_result: pass | fail` tag. Every later reader of this diagnostic — Phase 7's `evaluate.py::load_dev_oof_diagnostics`, `error_analysis.py` — fetches it by explicit `run_id` from this run, never from the local `reports/` mirror, which reflects whichever run last executed `threshold.py` on this machine, not necessarily the run being asked about.

**Deliverables:**

*Configs:* `configs/calibration/default.yaml` (`method: sigmoid | isotonic | auto`, fold counts, ECE binning, **`golden_n_rows`** — rows in the golden-parity fixture, 5 by default), `configs/costs.yaml` (the three scenarios as data — conservative/base/optimistic), `configs/threshold/default.yaml` (`random_state` for the argmax-EV bootstrap; **`n_bootstrap`** — a separate 1,000-resample knob for the dev-OOF screen's V1/V2/V2b slicing, distinct from `costs.yaml`'s `argmax_ev_bootstrap_n_samples`; **`v3_top_k_features`**/**`v3_min_direction_magnitude`** — the binding V3 cutoff and magnitude floor, dev-derived), `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` (generated output, not hand-authored).

*Source:*
- `src/telco_churn/models/calibrate.py` — method selection, logging (no registration), `select_golden_rows`, the dev-side SHAP summary, per the steps above. No `shap`/matplotlib import beyond what it already needs — the SHAP *computation* lives in `models/shap_values.py` (new; the `CalibratedClassifierCV` unwrap plus the `shap` call, shared with `error_analysis.py`'s test-side pass), and `calibrate.py` calls it once, in memory, so no deserialization round trip can put V3's verdict on a subtly different model than the one promoted.
- `src/telco_churn/models/threshold.py` — closed-form `t*`, the argmax-EV agreement check, the `r`-sensitivity sweep, and — its own last two steps — the pre-seal dev-OOF screen (`build_dev_oof_screen_frame`, `compute_dev_oof_diagnostics`, calibration-slope + V3). No longer leak-free by the *absence* of a `data.split` import (the screen needs the `dev` partition's segment columns); it remains leak-free by construction in the sense that matters — no `.fit(` call anywhere, and the one `data.split.partition()` call is restricted to `dev`, never `test`. Owns `load_policy_thresholds`/`resolve_policy_scenarios`/`resolve_policy_thresholds_by_scenario` and `CostScenario`/`load_costs_config`/`costs_config_hash` — the by-path policy-config layer, extracted into pure **`models/policy_config.py`** (imported back by `threshold.py`, `evaluate.py`, and Phase 9's serving layer, none of which need matplotlib to read a threshold).
- `src/telco_churn/models/diagnostics.py` — gains the shared V1/V2/V2b surface this step and Phase 7's `evaluate.py` both call: public `ROBUSTNESS_AXES`/`FAIRNESS_AXES` constants and `build_segment_lookup` (promoted out of what was a private, evaluate.py-local helper), plus `sliced_ranking_metrics`/`flag_segment_collapse`, `sliced_decision_rates`/`equal_opportunity_difference_by_axis`/`demographic_parity_difference_by_axis`, and `sliced_calibration`/`flag_calibration_collapse`. Full description under Phase 7's `diagnostics.py` bullet, since `evaluate.py`'s sealed-test reporting slices are this surface's other caller.
- `src/telco_churn/models/gate.py` — `slope_passes` (renamed from private `_slope_passes`) is now public specifically so this phase's dev-OOF screen can reuse the exact band check `gate.py`'s own cold-start/comparative decisions apply, rather than a second copy of the same tolerance-region logic. Stdlib-only (no I/O, no `cfg`) — the pure-decision module every other extraction below routes to, never the reverse.
- `src/telco_churn/models/calibration_metrics.py` (new, pure) — `pooled_brier`, `brier_skill_score`, `expected_calibration_error`, `murphy_decomposition`, `calibration_slope`, extracted out of `calibrate.py`; array→scalar only, no `cfg` parameter (callers pass `n_bins`/`strategy` explicitly, not the whole config).
- `src/telco_churn/models/artifacts.py` (new) — the read-what-an-upstream-stage-logged layer: `load_training_manifest`, `load_dev_features`, `load_dev_oof_predictions`, `load_fitted_model`, `load_threshold_validation`, `resolve_champion_version` — the layer that makes receipt-based resolution (below) work, shared by `calibrate.py`/`threshold.py`/`evaluate.py`/`error_analysis.py`.
- `src/telco_churn/models/plots.py` — pure plotting-data helpers (reliability-diagram bins); no rendering, no matplotlib import. Cost/EV curves and the sensitivity sweep stay in `threshold.py` itself, since it needs them internally.
- `src/telco_churn/features/accessor.py` — `load_features()`, the single accessor owning the processed-features path, format, and `sha256` content hash (`features_sha256()`, consumed by `models/train/tuning.py`'s study-key fix above); consumed by `calibrate.py`, `evaluate.py`, and `threshold.py`'s dev-OOF screen.
- `src/telco_churn/utils/mlflow.py` — `resolve_tracking_uri()` (as before), plus **`resolve_model_run_id`, `resolve_logged_model_id`, `load_model_promotion_bars`, and `ensure_experiment_metadata`**, all now shared here rather than living in `evaluate.py` (their original, single-caller home): `threshold.py`'s dev-OOF screen needs the same registry lookups `evaluate.py` needs, and importing them from `evaluate.py` would be circular, since `evaluate.py` already imports `CostScenario`/`costs_config_hash`/`load_costs_config` back from `threshold.py`. `ensure_experiment_metadata(cfg)` replaces the bare `mlflow.set_experiment(...)` call across every training-cycle module, setting an experiment-level description and tags once so the MLflow UI is self-documenting regardless of which module runs first.
- `src/telco_churn/utils/stats.py::paired_bootstrap_ci` — extracted from `models/train/comparison.py`, reused by `threshold.py`'s argmax-EV agreement check.

*Tests:* `tests/unit/test_calibrate.py`, `test_threshold.py`, `test_mlflow.py`, `test_accessor.py`; `tests/integration/test_calibrate_subprocess.py`, `test_threshold_subprocess.py` — leak canary (preprocessor refit count under `ensemble=False`), run-twice determinism, an AST/grep-style scan proving the module's leak-free claims structurally rather than by convention (`test_threshold_dev_oof_screen_has_no_refit_or_reslope_machinery` — the dev-OOF screen never re-fits the calibrated pipeline or re-derives a slope `calibrate.py` already computed), an inherited-contamination canary (the argmax-EV check passes on OOF probabilities and fails on in-sample ones), and hermetic dev-features fixtures. For the dev-OOF screen specifically: `test_run_threshold_step_screens_dev_oof_slope_and_writes_reports` (asserts `reports/dev_oof_predictions.parquet` + `reports/dev_oof_diagnostics.json` are written and match what's logged to MLflow) and `test_run_threshold_step_raises_on_bad_slope_read_from_calibration_summary` (a planted out-of-band slope raises `RuntimeError` and leaves `promotion_status` at `pending` — never `rejected`, since no evaluation verdict was ever reached). The integration suite covers both CLIs' exit-0 and exit-1 paths (missing/invalid `configs/costs.yaml`, `r = 0`, `t* ≥ 1`) plus `test_threshold_main_cli_ships_policy_regardless_of_dev_oof_screen_verdict` (the policy YAML and MLflow artifacts are written even on a screen failure — the audit trail records the failing attempt rather than it vanishing) and `test_threshold_main_cli_exits_one_when_model_version_does_not_exist`.

*Notebook:* `notebooks/04-calibration-and-threshold.ipynb` — reliability diagrams before/after calibration, method-selection diagnostics, per-scenario cost breakdowns, threshold-by-scenario and expected-value curves, the retention-rate sensitivity plot, and the dev-OOF V1/V2/V2b screen panels (segment PR-AUC floors, per-group decision rates, per-group calibration slopes) — rendered from `reports/dev_oof_diagnostics.json`, never recomputed.

**Verification:** `uv run python -m telco_churn.models.calibrate calibration.run_id=<id>` fits, logs the calibrated pipeline (no registry version, no alias), and writes `calibration_summary.json` / `dev_oof_predictions.parquet` / `dev_shap_values.parquet` / `calibration/golden_predictions.json` / `reports/calibrate_receipt.json` onto that run. `uv run python -m telco_churn.models.threshold` (resolving the run from the receipt, or an explicit `threshold.run_id=<id>` override) writes `threshold/threshold.json` + `configs/policy/threshold.yaml`, with the base scenario's `t*` falling inside its own empirical argmax-EV bootstrap CI, **then runs the pre-seal screen as its own last step**: confirm the dev-OOF slope sits inside §0's [0.80, 1.25] band and V3 direction sanity passes (settling `ANALYSIS.md` §9 #4), with `reports/dev_oof_predictions.parquet` + `reports/dev_oof_diagnostics.json` written and V1/V2/V2b reported (never gating) alongside them. Screens, not the gate — the gate is the *sealed-test* slope, one of Phase 7's four criteria of record; both screens are higher-powered than their sealed-test counterparts (n = 5,634 vs. 1,409) though mildly optimistic, so they can reject early but never approve. A champion failing either, on the surface biased in its favour, must be re-calibrated — far better discovered here, with the seal intact, than after Phase 7 spends the one-time sealed-test evaluation on it. `uv run python -m telco_churn.models.register` mints the version from `calibrate_receipt.json`, tags it `promotion_status: pending`, and points `challenger` at it — the only registration in the cycle. Full `pytest` green. See `ANALYSIS.md` §5–§6 for the recorded numbers and rationale (sigmoid selected via `isotonic_disqualified_pr_auc_gate`, BSS 0.31, `t* = 0.3941`, and §6's dev-OOF screen results: 0 flagged on V1/V2b, V2 flagging `seniorcitizen`/`has_partner`/`dependents` against pre-existing churn-rate gaps in the population rather than proxy discrimination) and `CHANGELOG.md` for delivery history.

---

### Phase 7 — Evaluation + Error Analysis + Registry Promotion *(2–3 days)*

**What this achieves:** A sealed test-set evaluation (touched here, once) produces bootstrap-CI-bounded metrics that honestly estimate production performance. A structured promotion gate — specified in `ANALYSIS.md` §0, not restated here — decides on those metrics for the first time, in its cold-start regime (no incumbent `champion`, so the absolute bars apply). A pass flips `champion` directly onto the registered version the metrics were recorded against — nothing is refit.

**This phase begins where Phase 6 leaves off, not from a clean slate.** `threshold.py`'s pre-seal dev-OOF screen (Phase 6's last step) has already run and passed — there is no standalone `calibration_screen.py` module here (an earlier draft of this plan put the screen in this phase; it moved to Phase 6, since its entire value is rejecting a model *before* the seal breaks). `evaluate.py` reads Phase 6's V1/V2/V2b diagnostics rather than recomputing them.

**Deliverables:**

*Configs:* `configs/model_promotion.yaml` — the five pre-registered `GateBars` (see `ANALYSIS.md` §0), loaded by path, never through Hydra composition. `configs/register/default.yaml` — `run_id`/`model_version` (mutually exclusive — the former mints, the latter acts on a verdict; neither inferred from the `challenger` alias), `require_review` (`true` here; Phase 10's monthly retrain sets it `false`), `alias` (parameterized so Phase 10's shadow/canary staging reuses `promote_to_alias` unchanged), `golden_atol`, `environment_packages`, `drift_reference_n_bins`. `configs/error_analysis/default.yaml`'s `top_k_shap_features` is renamed **`top_k_dependence_features`** — it now feeds test-side plots/metrics only, since the binding cutoff (`v3_top_k_features`) moved to `configs/threshold/default.yaml` in Phase 6.

*Source:*
- `src/telco_churn/models/gate.py` — `decide_promotion(candidate, incumbent, cfg) -> Decision`, a pure function implementing §0 (no I/O, no MLflow), returning `{regime, gate, criteria, model_version, metrics_content_hash}` — **no `review` field**. `evaluate.py` calls it once and logs the result as `promotion_decision.json`; `register.py` only re-reads the persisted verdict — two independent evaluations of one rule is how it becomes two rules. `GateInputs` carries interval-valued (Δ + CI) statistics, not bare scalars, since the comparative regime is a paired-bootstrap rule: a `(challenger_metric, champion_metric)` signature would silently collapse into `if new > old` and must never be "simplified" to one. `record_review(...)` writes the human verdict as its **own** append-only artifact (below) — a decision and an approval are two documents on two clocks, not one file with two authors. `check_threshold_provenance` (moved here from `evaluate.py` — pure, stdlib-only, reads nothing) also lives here.
- `src/telco_churn/models/evaluate.py` — the only module permitted to touch the sealed test set (`CLAUDE.md`'s modelling invariant); resolves the model by explicit `run_id` or version number, **never alias** — an alias is a moving pointer, and under Phase 10's monthly retrain `challenger` moves every cycle. One scoring pass computes: sealed-test PR-AUC/ROC-AUC against the Dummy floor; a purpose-built classification summary (not `sklearn.classification_report`, whose negative-class/weighted averages mislead at ~27% prevalence) at all three shipped thresholds with confusion counts; the fixed-recall profile; calibration (BSS, ECE, Murphy's decomposition, and the sealed-test **calibration slope — the one `gate.py` reads**, distinct from Phase 6's dev-OOF screen slope); decile lift/gains; business-impact EV per scenario with bootstrap CIs *and* the three-scenario parameter-uncertainty bracket kept as a separate, un-conflated error bar (`ANALYSIS.md` §0), plus a `capacity_budget_check` per scenario against `configs/costs.yaml`'s `contact_capacity`/`campaign_budget` (diagnostic, never gating); sensitivity sweeps and a tornado on the base scenario; and sliced fairness/robustness test metrics, reported only — V1/V2/V2b were already screened in Phase 6 and are read here (`load_dev_oof_diagnostics`), never recomputed. Writes `test_predictions.parquet`, `metrics.json`, `economics.json`, `promotion_decision.json`, and `reports/eval_receipt.json` (its own bootstrap pointer for `register.py`'s first pass). MLflow: its own `evaluation` run, metrics logged against `model_id` + a named `sealed_test` dataset (MLflow 3's native linkage), cost params as run params tagged with `costs_config_hash`. **No model-version tags written here** — `register.py` owns every registry tag write (below).
- `src/telco_churn/models/review.py` (new) — the sole CLI that produces `promotion_review.json`: `python -m telco_churn.models.review review.verdict=approved|rejected review.approver="..." review.notes="..."`, all three hard-required. Calls `gate.py::record_review(...)`, which appends `{verdict, notes, approver, reviewed_at}` to the eval run's `promotion_review.json["entries"]` — append-only, since a fixed-path `log_dict` overwrite would let a later `approved` silently erase an earlier `rejected` stamp. Jupyter-independent; the notebook's closing review cell is deleted, not re-pointed — it renders diagnostics only.
- `src/telco_churn/models/drift_reference.py` — pure builder, `build_reference(features_df, oos_proba, y, n_bins=10) -> dict` (per-feature bin edges/frequencies, reference score distribution, reference prevalence). `register.py` calls it with the **out-of-sample union of dev-OOF and sealed-test predictions** — never the champion's in-sample scores, which would bake in a permanent phantom drift signal.
- `src/telco_churn/models/register.py` — the single entry point for every registry write in the cycle, run as its own CLI step after `calibrate.py` has exited. **Mint:** given `register.run_id` (or `calibrate_receipt.json`), `register_challenger` mints the version, tags `promotion_status: pending`, points `challenger` at it. **Act:** given `register.model_version`, resolves `eval_run_id` (from the version's own tag, or `eval_receipt.json` on the first pass — tagged immediately either way) and reads `promotion_decision.json`; a gate failure vetoes unconditionally, checked *before* human review, so no review verdict can override it and none is required to exist for an already-rejected candidate. Only if the gate passed does it enforce `require_review`, reading `promotion_review.json["entries"][-1]` — `rejected` and a gate failure both route through the same `_tag_rejected` path. On a pass: independent re-check of the dev-OOF screen (calibration slope + V3, never trusted indirectly from `threshold.py`), asserts the four per-cycle manifest artifacts, resolves `error_analysis_run_id` the same tag-or-receipt way and aborts without it, then the two-phase serving smoke check (environment-parity, then schema/output-range/golden-parity **by explicit version, before the alias moves** — only on a pass does it flip `champion` and re-confirm parity **through** the alias, rolling back on failure) → `drift_reference.json` + `model_card.json` logged onto the promoted run → tag `promoted`, log `model_promoted`. **Also tags every version it processes — promoted or rejected — with `eval_run_id`, the four gate criteria (`test_pr_auc`/`test_recall`/`test_brier`/`test_calibration_slope`), and `error_analysis_run_id`**: `evaluate.py`/`error_analysis.py` write no registry tags of their own, so a rejected candidate's numbers stay legible to someone scanning the version list, not only a promoted one's. `rollback_champion()` (highest version tagged `promoted`, never `N−1`) is the single implementation both the post-flip failure path and any manual emergency rollback share. `model_card.json` is assembled strictly from artifacts already on record (`training_manifest.json`, `metrics.json`, `economics.json`, `error_analysis.json`) — baselined against `treat-none`/`treat-all` and the prevalence floor, never LogReg (a Phase 5 family-comparison candidate, not a champion rival) — never hand-transcribed.
- `src/telco_churn/models/diagnostics.py` — gains the shared V1/V2/V2b slicing surface (`build_segment_lookup`, `sliced_ranking_metrics`/`flag_segment_collapse`, `sliced_decision_rates` + the equal-opportunity/demographic-parity differences, `sliced_calibration`/`flag_calibration_collapse`), called by both Phase 6's dev-OOF screen (screened) and this phase's sealed-test slices (reported only — dev-OOF carries ~4× the churners per slice, so screening happens there, not here).
- `src/telco_churn/models/economics.py` — pure business-economics helpers (no I/O, no sklearn): `expected_value`, `campaign_cost`, `ev_by_k`, `break_even_retention_rate`, `capacity_budget_check`, the one-/two-way sensitivity sweeps, `tornado`. Reuses Phase 6's `CostScenario`/EV formula rather than a second definition of `p·r·LTV − c`.
- `src/telco_churn/models/explain.py` — pure SHAP helpers (no I/O, no `shap` import): `global_importance` (model documentation, not error analysis), `dependence_points` (the instrument behind direction sanity — the only thing that shows *direction*, which mean |SHAP| cannot), `direction_sanity_check` (V3's judge — Phase 6's `threshold.py` is now V3's binding caller; this module's test-side use is reported only), `cohort_shap` (signed, not absolute), `binary_feature_effects`, `local_explanations` (illustrative only, never evidence).
- `src/telco_churn/models/error_analysis.py` — runs after `evaluate.py`; owns `reports/error_analysis.json`, which `register.py` aborts without. Error-concentration scan (all features, not just the seven pre-registered axes) on dev-OOF only — confirmatory, never generative, and never on the 1,409-row sealed test. Error-confidence (near-miss vs. confident-failure) and value-weighted (`MonthlyCharges` decile) profiles on the sealed test. Cohort-level signed SHAP beyond a single FN-vs-TP mean. Re-audits Phase 6's binding V3 feature set on the **test** side via `models/shap_values.py`'s shared extraction — `direction_sanity_check_test`, reported only, never binding (a dev-pass/test-fail pair is a generalisation signal V3 alone can't give). Its own `error_analysis` MLflow run, persisting `shap_values.parquet` (not only the rendered charts) — `register.py` tags the model version `error_analysis_run_id` from this run, not `error_analysis.py` itself.

*Tests:* `tests/unit/test_evaluate.py`, `test_gate.py`, `test_diagnostics.py`, `test_stats.py`, `test_economics.py`, `test_explain.py`, `test_drift_reference.py`, `test_register.py` (17 tests), `test_error_analysis.py` + `tests/integration/test_error_analysis_subprocess.py` — the load-bearing cases: a challenger that ranks better but calibrates worse improves its Brier yet is vetoed by the calibration-slope guardrail; `sliced_decision_rates` catches a fairness gap that identical per-group PR-AUC hides; `ev_by_k`'s peak lands exactly at `t* = c/(r×LTV)`, a free cross-check of the whole cost model; `register.py`'s emergency-rollback fixture (a legitimate `promoted` champion at version M vs. a later, higher-numbered `rejected` challenger) proves `rollback_champion()` resolves by tag, never by version arithmetic; and a perturbed `golden_predictions.json` makes registration abort, proving the round trip is genuine rather than circular. Structural guards: `grep -r "data.split" src/telco_churn/models/error_analysis.py src/telco_churn/models/explain.py notebooks/05-evaluation-and-error-analysis.ipynb` returns nothing, and the notebook contains no `shap.` call and no model `.fit(`/`.predict`.

*Notebook:* `notebooks/05-evaluation-and-error-analysis.ipynb` — a pure renderer; computes and stamps nothing. Renders `metrics.json` and `error_analysis.json`, including Phase 6's dev-OOF V1/V2/V2b panels (not recomputed). The human-review interface is `models/review.py` alone, not this notebook — no Jupyter kernel is required to record a promotion verdict. Phase 10's unattended monthly retrain runs `error_analysis.py` and skips both the notebook and the review CLI (`register.require_review=false`) — only the review is human, and only it is skippable.

**Within-phase order: `evaluate` → `error_analysis` → `review` → `register`**, enforced structurally, not by convention: `evaluate.py` persists the gate's verdict to `promotion_decision.json`; `review.py` appends the human verdict to its own `promotion_review.json`; `register.py` checks the gate first (an unconditional veto — no review can override a gate failure) and, only if it passed, requires an approved review entry too. For this first promotion the review is a genuine second gate, not just confirmation — subgroup FN/FP breakdown and SHAP sanity can veto a model the automated gate already admitted.

**Verification:** `uv run python -m telco_churn.models.evaluate evaluate.run_id=<calibrate-run-id>` (or `evaluate.model_version=`, mutually exclusive) produces `metrics.json`/`test_predictions.parquet`/`promotion_decision.json` (`{regime, gate, criteria, model_version, metrics_content_hash}` — no `review` field) and `reports/eval_receipt.json`, with sealed-test PR-AUC/ROC-AUC CIs overlapping `README.md` and BSS/ECE comparable to Phase 6's dev-set figures. `uv run python -m telco_churn.models.review review.verdict=approved review.approver="..." review.notes="..."` appends to `promotion_review.json`'s `entries` list — `verdict`/`approver`/`notes` are all hard-required (a blank `notes` is refused, not silently accepted). `uv run python -m telco_churn.models.register register.run_id=<calibrate-run-id>` mints the challenger and tags it `pending`; `uv run python -m telco_churn.models.register register.model_version=<n>` then checks the gate (vetoes unconditionally on failure, before review is even read), then review, tags the version (`eval_run_id`, the four gate criteria, `error_analysis_run_id`) whether promoted or rejected, and — only on a full pass — runs the two-phase serving check (golden parity by version, then through the alias) before flipping `champion`, logging `drift_reference.json`/`model_card.json` onto the promoted run. A `rejected` review entry, a gate failure, or a smoke-check failure all tag `promotion_status: rejected` rather than leaving the version at `pending`. After registration, `telco-churn-pipeline` holds one version tagged `promotion_status: promoted`, carrying `champion` and its sealed-test metrics as `test_*` tags. Full `pytest` green. See `ANALYSIS.md` §6–§8 for the recorded verdict and `CHANGELOG.md` for delivery history.

---

### Phase 8 — DVC Pipeline Wrap *(3–5 days)*

**What this achieves:** The straight-line, file-producing half of the modelling lifecycle — `ingest → validate → split → features → train → calibrate → threshold → evaluate → error_analysis`, nine stages — becomes a content-hashed DAG: `dvc repro` reruns exactly the stages a change actually invalidates, nothing more. Registration (`register.py`'s mint/tag/flip) stays outside the DAG on principle, not oversight — `outs:` must be files, and a registry version plus an alias flip is neither; it mutates shared, external, pointer-based state that `dvc repro --force` cannot safely replay. A full manual cycle is therefore two `dvc repro` calls with `register.py`'s mint step spliced between them: `dvc repro calibrate`, then `register.py register.run_id=...` to mint the challenger version `threshold`/`evaluate`/`error_analysis` need to resolve their model against, then `dvc repro error_analysis` to finish the chain — not because the DAG is split, but because those three stages resolve their model by registered version rather than by touching the live `challenger`/`champion` alias, which no DAG stage may read or write. `CLAUDE.md`'s Model Registry section covers the full five-step cycle and `register.py`'s CLI dispatch in detail; this phase only wires the file-producing half into DVC.

**Deliverables:**

*Configs:* `dvc.yaml` — nine stages, each declaring `deps` (code + data files, curated per-file rather than whole packages or import closures, enforced by a coverage test), `params` (named `cfg.<key>` reads, never whole config directories — four categories are structurally exempt: env-resolved keys, identity tokens like `run_id`/`model_version`, the whole `paths.*` prefix, and `mlflow.experiment_name`; see `CLAUDE.md`'s DVC Pipeline section), and `outs`/`metrics`/`plots` (small, deterministic, local-inspection artifacts only — the model, `training_manifest.json`, SHAP values, and every other evidentiary artifact stays an MLflow run artifact resolved by `run_id`, never a DVC out). `.dvcignore`/`.gitignore` — `reports/figures/` (four stages share the directory; MLflow already owns every figure per-run), `reports/validation/` (timestamped, failure-only evidence dirs), `mlruns/`.

*Source:*
- `models/register.py` — gains the mint step (`register_challenger`, extracted out of `calibrate.py`): mints the version, tags it `promotion_status: pending` immediately (before any downstream step can fail), and points `challenger` at it. Its `__main__` gains a second, mutually exclusive dispatch mode — `register.run_id=` mints, `register.model_version=` promotes/rejects — making it the single entry point for every registry write in the cycle.
- `models/calibrate.py` — drops its registration call entirely; fits and logs the calibrated pipeline only, writing `reports/calibrate_receipt.json` as the bootstrap pointer every downstream stage resolves its model from.
- `models/threshold.py`, `models/evaluate.py`, `models/error_analysis.py` — resolve their model by `run_id` (read from the upstream receipt) rather than a hardcoded registry version, so `dvc.yaml`'s static `cmd` strings keep working across cycles; `evaluate.py` additionally takes an explicit `champion_version` override instead of reading the live `champion` alias inside a DAG stage, closing an undeclared dependency on mutable registry state.
- `data/validate.py` — `write_validation_receipt()`, an unconditional writer (pass or fail, no timestamp, deterministic bytes) that becomes the DVC `validate` out; `save_validation_report()` is untouched and stays an untracked, timestamped debugging mirror. `CleanedSchema`/`validate_clean` deleted as unreachable once imputation moved into the model pipeline, after promoting its one live invariant into `RawSchema`.
- `features/build.py` — its `__main__` becomes the `features` stage entry point: writes `datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet` (Parquet, not CSV) and raises on a zero-row result rather than writing a silent empty artifact. `features/accessor.py::load_features()` repointed at the same file.
- `models/train/common.py` — `data_content_hash` (a real `sha256` of the features parquet via `features_sha256()`) replaces the retired `_dvc_hash`, which read a `.dvc` sidecar that never exists for a pipeline-stage output and silently fell back to `"unknown"`. The Optuna study key (`tuning.py::_study_name()`) is completed with the tuning metric/direction/sampler seed so a data or objective change can never silently resume an incompatible study; a complete study is always reused, a partial one is re-searched unless `tuning.resume=true`.
- `tests/unit/test_architecture.py` — structural guards: every stage's `params:`/`deps:` cover the real `cfg.` reads and import closure of its own modules (curated exclusions require an explicit exempt marker); every `paths.*` key is bound to the document that actually restates its path (`dvc.yaml`, or `.gitignore`/`.dvcignore`); the sealed-test partition stays unreachable from `error_analysis.py`/`explain.py`/notebook 05 by transitive import closure, not a one-hop grep.

*Tests:* the guard tests above, receipt-determinism tests (byte-identical output across two runs on identical input) for `ingest`/`validate`, and updated subprocess tests for `calibrate.py`/`threshold.py`/`evaluate.py`/`error_analysis.py` covering the `run_id`-based resolution path.

**Verification:** `dvc dag` renders the nine-stage graph. Changing a hyperparameter in `configs/training/lightgbm.yaml` reruns `train` through `error_analysis` only, leaving `ingest`/`validate`/`split`/`features` cached; changing `configs/costs.yaml` reruns `threshold`/`evaluate`/`error_analysis` only; raising a bar in `configs/model_promotion.yaml` reruns `threshold`/`evaluate` — the negative case a blanket config-directory dep could never demonstrate. `dvc repro --force` on unchanged deps completes with `n_trials_run_this_invocation == 0` and an unchanged selected trial, confirming Optuna study reuse is genuine rather than assumed. A full manual cycle — `dvc repro calibrate` → `register.py register.run_id=<id>` → `dvc repro error_analysis` → `models.review` → `register.py register.model_version=<n>` — produces a promoted or rejected version exactly as `CLAUDE.md`'s five-step cycle specifies. `dvc metrics diff main` and `dvc plots diff main` surface the gate's numbers and the decile-lift curve as the PR-facing signal; this project deliberately does not gate CI on a `dvc.lock` byte-diff, since `train`/`calibrate`'s receipts are identity tokens that change on every genuine execution rather than derived content, making such a diff report a rerun rather than a regression. `uv run pytest` green, including the new architecture guards. See `ANALYSIS.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` for delivery history across the prerequisite and wrap PRs.

### Phase 9 — FastAPI Serving + Streamlit UI *(3 days)*

**What this achieves:** The champion model is accessible via a REST API with hot-reload (a promotion is just an MLflow alias flip; no redeploy needed), a capacity-constrained contact policy, and a config-gated shadow/canary rollout mechanism. A Streamlit UI lets a non-technical user look up an existing customer or run a what-if scenario, both calling the same API a real client would use.

**Deliverables:**

*Configs:* `configs/serving/api.yaml` — poll interval, `shadow.enabled`/`canary.enabled`+`fraction` (both off by default), `explanation.top_k`, `auth.enabled` (off by default), `batch.max_size` (500).

*Source:*
- `models/policy_config.py::expected_value_per_customer`, `serving/contact_policy.py::select_contacts` — capacity-constrained contact policy: `decision = proba >= t*` is computed once and never touched by capacity; `contact` ranks the `decision`-true set by expected value and cuts at `min(contact_capacity, floor(campaign_budget / cost))`. Two separate output fields, never one flag reinterpreted per branch.
- `serving/schemas.py` — Pydantic v2 request/response models. `customerid` is a pass-through identifier, never a model feature, stripped before `predict_proba`. `decision`/`contact` are `bool` on the wire, never a string.
- `serving/predict.py` — loads the champion at startup; `resolve_serving_model()` TTL-polls `champion`/`challenger`, diffing the target version's logged dependencies against what's installed (`environment_parity.py`) before hot-reloading — fail-open on a refresh problem (keep serving the last-known-good bundle), fail-closed on the very first load. `resolve_champion_model`/`resolve_challenger_model` are the single indirection point; no call site resolves `models:/…@champion` directly. Shadow scores every request against the challenger for comparison but never serves it; canary routes a consistent-hash slice of traffic (salted by the challenger's own `model_version`, so the canary population reshuffles per version) — independent, config-gated toggles, not one flag with two readings. Five Prometheus metrics instrumented at the same point the structured log entry is written. SHAP explainer and threshold policy reload in lockstep with the model inside one `ModelBundle`, never cached independently.
- `serving/app.py` — FastAPI app: `POST /predict` (probability + optional SHAP explanation, against the pre-calibration score), `POST /predict/batch` (three item shapes — ID-only, full inline, ID-plus-override — partial success with per-row `validation_error`/`resolution_error`, 500-row cap → `413`), `GET /customer/{id}` (raw fields, `churn` excluded, `404` on miss), `GET /health` (liveness only), `GET /ready` (`model_version`/`run_id`, `503` before first load, flips to `503` on SIGTERM ahead of a graceful drain), `GET /metrics`. Optional API-key auth (`require_api_key`, constant-time compare), applied to the three data routes only — never `/health`/`/ready`/`/metrics`. Routes ship unversioned this phase (`/predict`, not `/predict/v1`) — adopt `/v1` no later than Phase 11, before an external consumer exists to break. Rate limiting and a request-body-size ceiling are named Phase 12 prerequisites, not built here.
- `serving/customer_lookup.py` — async ID-resolution against `customers_crm` (not `customers_raw` — see the `crm_data.py` bullet below) shared by `GET /customer/{id}` and batch ID-resolution; a row missing a required field is resolved by `customerid`, then any caller-supplied fields override the looked-up base.
- **`serving/crm_data.py`, `sql/schema/003_create_customers_crm.sql`** — gap-fill, added just after Phase 9 shipped: `customers_crm`, a seeded (`random_state: 42`) "current state" derivation of `customers_raw` (tenure +1–6 months, contract upgrade-only, `totalcharges` accrues, everything else fixed), so live lookups have a source genuinely distinct from the frozen training snapshot instead of silently reusing it. `make crm-data` populates it — safe to re-run, deterministic under the fixed seed.
- `ui/streamlit_app.py` — **Score a Customer** tab (Fetch a real customer from `customers_crm` to prefill, or fill in the form manually; editing a fetched customer's fields is a legitimate what-if), **Batch Prediction** tab (CSV upload mapped into `/predict/batch`'s three item shapes, capped at `batch.max_size`), **Model Info** tab (renders `model_card.json` off the champion's own run, graceful degradation if missing). Widgets sourced from `features/schema.py`'s constraint sets, never a hand-typed option list.
- `models/environment_parity.py` — `diff_environment`, the hot-reload dependency-compatibility check `predict.py`'s poll loop and `register.py`'s pre-promotion smoke check both call.
- `docker/api/Dockerfile`, `docker/ui/Dockerfile` — multi-stage, built from the project's own `uv.lock`, never `pyproject.toml` ranges resolved fresh at build time. `docker-compose.yml` gains `fastapi`/`streamlit` services, unprofiled so a bare `docker compose up -d` brings up the full stack; `postgres`/`mlflow` lose their `profiles: [infra]` gate to make that `depends_on` edge valid — infra-only startup is now `docker compose up -d postgres mlflow`.

*Tests:* `tests/unit/test_predict.py`, `test_contact_policy.py`, `test_schemas.py`, `test_environment_parity.py`; `tests/integration/test_api.py` (`TestClient`), `test_predict_subprocess.py` (a real uvicorn subprocess over a real socket — caught a real bug where `lifespan()` awaited the initial model load directly, leaving `/health` unreachable, not just `/ready`, until a champion existed), `test_serving_parity.py` (an ID-only `/predict/batch` request must score identically to an inline `/predict` request built from that same customer's current `GET /customer/{id}` row — a self-consistency check on the Postgres lookup → `_assemble_model_input` → `predict_proba` chain, not a bit-exact match against `calibrate.py`'s frozen `golden_predictions.json`, since `customers_crm`'s seeded nudge deliberately diverges from the training-time values that reference was computed against); `tests/streamlit/test_streamlit_app.py` (headless `AppTest` smoke suite). `make smoke-test-serving` scripts a `docker compose up` + `curl /ready`/`/predict`/`/customer/<id>` check.

**Verification:** `docker compose up -d --build` brings all four services to `healthy`; `/ready` returns `503` then `200` with `model_version`/`run_id` populated. `/health` stays `200` even with Postgres stopped (liveness only). `/predict` returns a probability with a 5-entry SHAP explanation; `/customer/<id>` returns a real customer's raw fields. A `/predict/batch` call against a capacity-forced fixture returns `decision`/`contact`/`capacity_limit` exactly as the ranking rule specifies. `/metrics` exposes all five custom metrics with non-zero values after driving traffic. Streamlit's Score a Customer, Batch Prediction, and Model Info tabs all verified against the live stack; `tests/streamlit/test_streamlit_app.py` passes headless. Full mechanism (hot-reload, shadow/canary routing, Prometheus labelling) documented with a real log/metrics snippet in `docs/architecture.md`, not a screenshot. See `CHANGELOG.md` `[0.9.0]` for delivery history, `[0.9.1]` for the `customers_crm` gap-fill.
