Not redundant with `performance_monitor.py`: for these two reserve months the champion's numbers there and here largely coincide (the reserve cohort *was* served through the real path, so the champion's reserve metrics are realised, not a re-backtest — only the candidate is backtested), but `performance_monitor.py` is the general continuous champion-health loop while this is the specific paired-with-candidate slice, and they stay consistent by construction. **`comparative_deltas`' persisted output also gains a bootstrap `se` per delta on the same pass** — consumed by `gate.py::decide_review_escalation` for the gate-boundary check; see the `require_review` bullet's "persist a real bootstrap `se` per criterion" sub-point.
  >   - **`error_analysis.py` on a routine cycle — the sealed-test SHAP pass moves to the reserve cohort, the error-concentration scan stays on dev-OOF, and two "never override the sealed-test lookup" rules invert for this path.** Everything in `run_error_analysis_step` that today reads `y_test`/`proba_test` from `reports/test_predictions.parquet` — `_compute_shap_diagnostics` (SHAP computed once → global importance, dependence, binary effects, `shap_values.parquet`), `_compute_cohort_gap` ×2 (FN-vs-TP and FP-vs-TN signed-SHAP gaps, each independently ranked/elbow-checked), `_annual_contract_subgroup_finding`, `_compute_illustrative_cases` (FN/FP/TP/TN waterfalls), `_compute_error_profiles` (error-confidence + value-weighted), and the reported-only `direction_sanity_check_test` re-audit of `threshold.py`'s V3 dev-side audit set — runs instead on the candidate's **reserve-cohort** scored output. `performance_check.py` already writes that parquet to the same fixed `reports/test_predictions.parquet` path once Step 0 is dropped (`customerid` / `y_true=churned` / `p_hat=candidate reserve proba` / `logged_model_id`), so `_load_error_analysis_inputs` reads it unchanged and its `logged_model_id` staleness check still holds — only the rows' population changes, and the `y_test`-named locals stay (a naming choice on scored output, per `CLAUDE.md`'s test-set invariant note). **Two rules invert:** `_features_by_customer_id`'s *"sealed-test lookups never pass `raw_partition_override`"* and `_load_error_analysis_inputs`'s *"threaded only to the dev-OOF lookup, never the sealed-test one"* — on a routine cycle the reserve customers' engineered feature values must come from `training_pool` / `customer_features` (the nudged values the model actually scored), not `load_features()`'s frozen `customers_raw` snapshot, so the override (or an equivalent `reserve_month`-scoped `customer_features` read) feeds **both** lookups. **The error-concentration scan (`error_concentration_scan` ×2, ranked by FNR then FPR) stays on dev-OOF** — it chose dev-OOF over the 1,409-row sealed set for slicing power, and a ~tens-of-churners reserve month is strictly worse; on a routine cycle its population is the training-pool OOF (`dev ∪ matured reserve`), exactly where 10a-ii's `raw_partition_override` for the dev-OOF feature lookup already applies unchanged. So routine-cycle error analysis is deliberately a **mix** — SHAP / cohort gaps / illustrative cases / error profiles on `reserve_month N`, concentration scan on `dev ∪ matured reserve` OOF — and `error_analysis.json` carries a per-block population field so `model_card.json` assembly and a human reviewer can see which is which; do not "consistency-fix" it to one population. **Thin-support handling:** at that *n* the FN/TP and FP/TN subsets `_compute_cohort_gap` splits into can be single digits, `check_top_k_elbow` will routinely warn, and `_illustrative_indices` may not find `n_local_explanation_examples` FN/FP cases (already degrades — empty grid panels turned off); `_annual_contract_subgroup_finding` conditions further to near-empty and should be **skipped with a logged reason below a support floor** rather than emit a Mann-Whitney p-value on n<10. `error_analysis.json` carries explicit per-block n's so a thin finding is never rendered in the model card as if it were solid. The **binding** subgroup/fairness read (V1/V2/V2b) is untouched — it lives in `evaluate.py`/`diagnostics.py` on dev-OOF (`ANALYSIS.md` §9 #11), not here. **Cold start is unchanged** (`raw_partition_override=None`, whole pass on the sealed test set). **A rare new-generation cycle** runs the SHAP pass on the sealed test set (that is what its `evaluate.py` gate scored) but threads `raw_partition_override` to the **dev-OOF** lookup only — it too trains on `dev ∪ matured reserve`, so its dev-OOF vector carries reserve customers with nudged `training_pool` values, the same reason the routine path needs it, applied to just the dev-OOF half.
  >   - **The four `test_*` model-version tags** (`test_pr_auc`/`test_recall`/`test_brier`/`test_calibration_slope`, written by `register.py`) — **kept, not removed**, but reframed. They stop being "always sealed-test" and become **this cycle's binding-surface metrics**: sealed-test values on a rare cycle, reserve-cohort values on a routine cycle. The `gate_source` tag (`"evaluate"` / `"performance_check"` / `"v1_cold_start"`, already planned in 10a-ii's `register.py` bullet) is the disambiguator that tells a registry reader which surface a given row's four numbers came from. **This retires the separate `gate_pr_auc_delta` half-measure** — instead the four tags carry the binding absolute values directly, plus per-criterion deltas vs. the incumbent on a routine cycle (`gate_pr_auc_delta`, `gate_recall_delta`, …). **The justification for the tags narrows from two reasons to one.** *Comparability* — "read v3's number against v4's down the version list" — genuinely does not survive: adjacent reserve months are different populations and a tag holds no CI, so that comparison moves to the `performance_monitor.py` / MLflow `(model, dataset)` dashboard, which is where population-matching and confidence intervals live and where industry has always put cross-version comparison. *Legibility under pressure* — "champion is misbehaving, I'm scanning versions and want v4's own gate result without opening a run" — is unaffected and is why the tags stay: a **per-version snapshot of that version's binding eval**, a standalone fact, never a series to read down.
  >   - **Code footprint** (built code with tests, not doc-only): `performance_check.py` loses `_score_sealed_test`, `_compute_sealed_test_metrics`, and the sealed-test halves of `_assemble_payloads` / `_log_performance_check_run`; `run_performance_check` drops its Step-0 branch and instead writes the reserve-cohort scored predictions to `reports/test_predictions.parquet`, adds the incumbent-champion `logged_model_id` metric logging (Q1 above), and computes reserve-cohort decile lift / EV / capacity check. `error_analysis.py`: `_load_error_analysis_inputs` and `_features_by_customer_id` thread `raw_partition_override` to the (now reserve-cohort) "test" lookup too, not only the dev-OOF one; `_annual_contract_subgroup_finding` gains a support-floor skip; `_assemble_error_analysis_payload` adds the per-block population field. `tests/unit/test_performance_check.py`, `tests/unit/test_error_analysis.py`, `tests/integration/test_performance_check_postgres.py` update accordingly. Cold start is fully untouched; a rare new-generation cycle threads `raw_partition_override` to `error_analysis.py`'s dev-OOF lookup only (its SHAP pass stays on the seal), and its data assembly moves from the DVC DAG to `assemble_training_bundle` — see the task-granularity bullet above.
  >
  > **`CLAUDE.md` ripples, all in the same PR:**
  >   - **Modelling Invariants** — the "Test set touched once" bullet: drop the clause added this session about `performance_check.py` still *scoring* the sealed set each routine cycle / the `sealed_test_diagnostic` block — a routine cycle now genuinely never touches the seal. Keep the routine/rare split and the "`reserve` cohort is this project's bounded proxy for the industry-standard rolling/walk-forward holdout" framing.
  >   - **MLflow Model Registry** — the "four gate criteria are also set as model-version tags … on every version" paragraph and its "Shipped in Phase 10a-ii" note: rewrite so the four tags are "the binding evaluation surface for this cycle, `gate_source` says which," not "always sealed-test, diagnostic-only on a routine cycle." Remove the `gate_pr_auc_delta`-as-sole-binding-number framing; replace with the four tags + per-criterion deltas.
  >   - **Evaluation (Phase 7 `evaluate.py`)** section — the note that `performance_check.py` also sets `test_*`-prefixed metrics needs a caveat that on a routine cycle those are the reserve cohort's, logged under `dataset="reserve_month_N"`, not the sealed test set's; and that the same run also carries the incumbent champion's reserve-cohort metrics under the champion's `logged_model_id` (the paired-measurement point above), an application of the section's own "persist the evidence, not just the conclusion" rule to `comparative_deltas`' inputs.
  >   - **`model_card.json` assembly** — "performance summary … from `metrics.json`" now means reserve-cohort performance for a routine-cycle promotion (so the card reflects drift); the fairness/limitations section is likewise reserve-cohort on a routine cycle, with the thin-support caveat.
  >
  > **`ANALYSIS.md` §9 #13 ripple:** the sentence added this session ("The routine cycle does still *score* the sealed test set each time …") must go — revise to state the routine cycle's evaluation surface is the reserve cohort alone, and the sealed set is touched only by rare `evaluate.py` cycles.
  >
  > `register.py`'s `metrics_content_hash` cross-check already tolerates a metrics population that differs from the decision's — `_assemble_payloads`'s own docstring notes the hash "does not require the decision to be about the same population `metrics.json` describes" — and after this change they *are* the same population again, so that note becomes moot rather than load-bearing. **Settle before `training_cycle.py` wires the module in.**
  > - **`register.py`'s model-card abort — regime-independent (applies to cold-start, rare, and routine cycles alike), scoped to champion promotion only, and put behind `register.require_model_card_completeness` (default `true`).** It belongs to `register.py`'s act step, which runs identically regardless of which gate produced the verdict (line above: *"`register.py`'s mint … act calls are each their own task/primitive regardless of regime"*); only the *contents* of `error_analysis.json` vary by regime, not the abort check. Phase 7 specifies `register.py` "aborts without" `error_analysis.json`; two clarifications this needs before `training_cycle.py` runs it unattended. **(1) Scope.** The abort fires only on the *champion-promotion act* path (where `model_card.json` is written once), never on challenger mint, and it gates on *"the error-analysis run exists and carries an `error_analysis.json` artifact"* — i.e. the analysis actually ran — **not** on whether every section is populated. Thin or skipped content inside a produced artifact (the reserve-cohort subgroup-finding-below-floor skip above; a `not_assessed` fairness slice) records its own `n`/status and does not abort, the same way `capacity_budget_check` already degrades to `None` rather than blocking. Conflating "thin data" with "broken pipeline" would let a documentation gap veto a gate-passing, human-approved model. **(2) Toggle.** `register.require_model_card_completeness` (`configs/register/default.yaml`, default `true`) makes the strictness a visible policy knob rather than a hardcoded assumption, so a fresh clone / disaster-recovery rebuild / an environment where `error_analysis.py` isn't yet wired isn't wedged by it. Default-on preserves the governance property — a champion cannot be promoted without a pipeline-generated error-analysis run behind its card, and hand-transcription stays forbidden regardless of the toggle — while matching how regulated model-risk pipelines (SR 11-7, EU AI Act Annex IV) actually ship this kind of gate: mandatory in normal operation, with a documented override path, not an unconditional hard stop. Industry outside regulated settings mostly does not gate the promotion *pipeline* on model-card completeness at all (the card is a required human-review artifact; the pipeline gates on metrics) — default-on here is deliberately stricter, kept as a governance-maturity demonstration.
  > - **⚠ Model-card quality upgrade — `model_card.json` (assembly in `register.py`) and its Streamlit "Model Info" rendering both fall short of governance-grade, and this is a 10b task because every retrain cycle regenerates the card unattended, so a gap in the template compounds silently.** Reviewed the shipped `reports/model_card.json` (v1) and `ui/streamlit_app.py::_render_about_model_tab` against Model Cards for Model Reporting (Mitchell et al. 2019), Google's Model Card Toolkit schema, the Hugging Face card spec, and EU AI Act Annex IV as the strict checklist. The card is already well above the typical bar (plain-language exec summary, explicit out-of-scope uses, sensitive-attributes-used disclosure with rationale, disaggregated metrics with CIs on robustness *and* fairness axes, per-segment calibration, transparent promotion criteria, provenance run IDs). The gaps:
  >   - **`register.py` model-card assembly.** **(1) Adjudicate the fairness flags — the biggest gap.** The card *flags* real disparities (`v2_equal_opportunity_flagged`: dependents 0.178 / has_partner 0.142 / seniorcitizen 0.144 on dev-OOF; up to 0.35 on the sealed test) but never records what was decided about them. Add a `fairness_assessment` block: per flagged axis — the disparity, **who is disadvantaged and how** (churning customers *with dependents* are missed 46–60% of the time vs. 25–28% without → fewer retention offers), disposition (`accepted` / `mitigated` / `blocked`), rationale, named owner, revisit date. **(2)** Change `ethical_considerations` "fairness is *enforced* by measuring" → "*monitored* by measuring," pointing at `fairness_assessment` (the card currently claims enforcement while showing unenforced flagged gaps). **(3)** `business_case.human_oversight` — who signs off on the generated contact list, at what cadence, what triggers a manual hold (Annex IV "human oversight measures"). **(4)** `card_metadata` block — `schema_version`, `generated_by: "register.py"`, `generated_at` (distinct from `promoted_at`), `supersedes` (prior card's run_id or null), `valid_as_of`, `review_cadence: "monthly retrain; off-cycle on drift/perf alarm"`. **(5)** `technical_appendix.training_data.composition` — training churn prevalence (26.5%, currently only stated elsewhere), per-fairness-axis group sizes, IBM Telco snapshot vintage, the "single static snapshot" note, key preprocessing (`TotalCharges` median impute, `class_weight='balanced'`). **(6)** `business_case.shipped_operating_point: "base"` + one-line why (`t* = c/(r×LTV)` under base cost assumptions); label conservative/optimistic as what-if. **(7)** Surface the budget/capacity position in `risks_and_limitations` **precisely — not as "this campaign is unaffordable."** The base plan is net-positive (retained revenue $44,875 − campaign cost $29,814 = EV $15,061; bootstrap CI [$11.2k, $18.6k] excludes zero; ~1.5× return; break-even retention rate 19.9%), so exceeding the *authorized* budget is a ceiling question, not a profitability one. But `capacity_budget_check.base.over_budget = true` / `budget_excess ≈ $14,814`: the base contact list (440 customers @ ~$67.76 each) needs ~2× the ~$15k configured `campaign_budget`, and the headline $15,061 is the EV of contacting all 440 — a spend the budget doesn't authorize. State (i) the ROI argues for raising the ceiling; (ii) if it holds, only the top ~221 by expected value are funded — `contact` already truncates to `min(contact_capacity, floor(campaign_budget / cost))`, the rest carry `decision: true, contact: false`. **(7a) Add an explicit `expected_impact.ev_at_configured_budget` field** — `ev_by_k` (logged in `economics.json`, `ev_by_k_logged: true`) evaluated at `k = floor(campaign_budget / cost_per_contact)`, per scenario — so the card shows the realized-at-budget EV next to the all-440 figure rather than leaving a reader to assume the headline is achievable. It is *higher* than `(k/n) × headline` (the truncation keeps the highest-EV contacts) but below the headline; naming both is the honest presentation. **(8)** Reconcile `promotion_rationale` (presents a merit-based cold-start promotion) vs `human_review.review_notes` ("Mechanism-verification cycle, not a genuine model-quality review," approver "Claude Code (automated local reinit)") — an auditor reads that pair as an unreviewed model in production, and today only the *prose* discloses it. Fix via the structured `review_kind` field defined in the `require_review` bullet below: when `human_review.review_kind == "automated_bootstrap"`, (i) the card carries `review_kind` in `governance.human_review`, (ii) `card_metadata.review_status: "automated_bootstrap"` is set top-level so it's filterable without opening the review block, (iii) `promotion_rationale` is generated as *"promoted without human review (automated bootstrap stamp) — see human_review"* rather than the merit narrative, and (iv) the Streamlit Model Info tab renders a distinct callout — *"⚠ Not human-reviewed. Promoted via an automated bootstrap stamp for pipeline verification. No person has assessed its fairness, calibration, or subgroup behavior."* — loudest when it coincides with a cold-start escalation (`review_escalation.reason == "cold_start regime — mandatory"`), where the missing-rollback-target risk is real. **(9)** `governance.feedback_and_escalation` — where to file a production concern, expected response, Phase 10 monitoring as the standing review mechanism. **(10)** One `known_limitations` line on training/serving skew — serving features come from `customers_crm` (simulated-forward snapshot), so a customer's serving-time values can differ from training-time. **(11) Bug — invalid JSON:** lines emit bare `NaN` (`"precision": NaN` in empty segments); `json.dump(..., allow_nan=False)` and map `NaN → null` with a sibling reason field. **(12)** Unify the key names `equal_opportunity_gap_by_axis`/`_flagged` (dev_oof) vs `equal_opportunity_difference_by_axis`/`equal_opportunity_diff` (sealed_test) — same concept, inconsistent keys break programmatic consumption. **(13, low-pri)** one-line compute/environmental footprint ("CPU-only, minutes-scale, negligible; not separately measured") for HF-spec completeness.
  >   - **`error_analysis.py` — fairness-assessment inputs.** So `register.py` *assembles* `fairness_assessment` rather than computing (or the UI retyping) it: `error_analysis.py` emits, per flagged fairness axis, the direction translation — which group carries the higher FNR / selection rate, by how much — into `error_analysis.json`'s fairness section. The human dispositions themselves (accept/mitigate/block + rationale + owner) are judgment calls and live in `ANALYSIS.md` §7/§9; `register.py` reads them from there (or a small `configs/` dispositions file) the same tag-or-artifact way it resolves everything else — never hand-transcribed into the card.
  >   - **`ui/streamlit_app.py` — Model Info tab (`_render_about_model_tab`).** **(a) Bug:** `_INTERNAL_REFERENCE_PATTERN` handles dotted paths only for `business_case.`, so `See technical_appendix.error_concentration for the full ranked list.` strips to `decisions.error_concentration for the full ranked list.` (regex stops at the first period, leaks a raw key). Fix: `(?:ANALYSIS\.md|README\.md|technical_appendix|training_manifest\.json|business_case|risks_and_limitations)(?:\.[\w]+)*[^.]*\.`; add a dotted-reference case to `test_streamlit_app_helpers.py`. **(b) Fairness Check** currently renders one vague sentence ("Potential fairness gaps flagged in: equal opportunity gap, demographic parity gap") — axis, magnitude, direction, and disposition all discarded, and the "reviewed, consistent with real risk differences, tracked not blocking" verdict exists *only in a code comment* (lines ~1061–1066). Render a small table (axis · gap · who's disadvantaged · disposition) + the verdict line — sourced from the card's new `fairness_assessment` block, not re-derived in the UI (re-deriving is the "retyped fairness section" anti-pattern `CLAUDE.md` forbids, moved to the UI layer). **(c) Expected Business Impact** shows three clean tiles with no budget-breach caveat, no realized-at-budget EV (the `ev_at_configured_budget` field from (7a)), no `ev_treat_none` ($0) / `ev_treat_all` (−$31,169) anchors, and no scenario range (conservative $11.7k / base $15.1k / optimistic $11.8k) — add all four, and reframe the headline tile so "over the authorized budget, still net-positive, ~221 of 440 funded this cycle" reads as the operational reality rather than an alarm. **(d)** The tab's own caption says "governance details" but renders none: add `human_review` (approver/date/notes), `promotion_context.criteria` vs bars + the dummy floor (0.265), and `card_metadata.review_cadence`/`valid_as_of`.
  >   - **Sequencing — three sub-tasks.** **(a) Land immediately, no dependency:** the `_INTERNAL_REFERENCE_PATTERN` regex fix + its test case. **(b) Land immediately, no dependency:** the Streamlit Business Impact additions (budget-position caveat, realized-at-budget EV, `ev_treat_none`/`ev_treat_all` anchors, scenario range) — `ev_by_k` and the scenario/treat-all values are already in the shipped `economics.json`/card JSON; the at-budget EV is a `floor(budget/cost)` index into the existing `ev_by_k` curve, no new computation. The card-side `ev_at_configured_budget` field (7a) is the only piece needing a `register.py` change, and it's a pure read of an already-logged array. **(c) Gated on the `register.py` assembly changes above:** the Streamlit Fairness Check table and the human-review / promotion-criteria / review-cadence surfaces — the app can only render what the card carries, and the fairness disposition text must originate in the artifact (`fairness_assessment` block), never be re-derived in the UI.
  > - **`require_review` becomes a computed per-cycle value, not the static `cfg.register.require_review` bool — resolved during 10b's suspend/review design discussion.** 10a-ii settled the *policy* (line 635: `evaluate.py` / cold-start cycles always escalate; routine `performance_check.py` cycles escalate only on a gate rejection or a gate-boundary pass within one bootstrap SE); this is the *mechanism*. **The constraint that forces the shape:** two consumers must never disagree — `training_cycle.py` decides whether to `pause_flow_run` *before* calling `register.py` act, and `register.py` act (`register.py:2212`) decides whether to demand a `promotion_review.json` entry. A split produces a hang (`register.py` raises `"No promotion_review.json entries found"` on a cycle the flow never suspended) or a silent drop (the flow records a `rejected` that `register.py` ignores under `require_review=false`). So the rule is **computed once, persisted, read by both — never recomputed on either side**, the same discipline `decide_promotion` already follows.
  >   - **`gate.py::decide_review_escalation(decision, gate_source, se_by_criterion, cfg) -> ReviewEscalation`** — new pure function, no I/O. `ReviewEscalation = {required: bool, mode: "veto" | "audit", reason: str, margin_in_se: float | None, boundary_criterion: str | None}`. `required` is `true` iff `gate_source in {"v1_cold_start", "evaluate"}` **or** `decision["gate"] == "fail"` **or** (`gate_source == "performance_check"` and some binding criterion's margin-to-threshold `< cfg.model_promotion.review_escalation.se_multiple` SEs, default `1.0`). `mode` is `"audit"` iff `decision["gate"] == "fail"` else `"veto"` — a pure function of the gate verdict, encoding line 636's asymmetry (on a rejection a human `approved` has no power → audit-only; on a boundary pass a human `rejected` overrides → veto). `required` layers regime + boundary on top of `mode`.
  >   - **Persisted in `promotion_decision.json["review_escalation"]`** — deliberately *not* `["review"]`: this is the machine's "does this need a human?" determination, as much part of the decision as `gate: pass`, whereas the human verdict stays in append-only `promotion_review.json`. The field's docstring says exactly that, so `CLAUDE.md`'s "no `review` field / a decision and an approval are two documents on two clocks" rule is untouched. `evaluate.py` and `performance_check.py` both call `decide_review_escalation` right after `decide_promotion` and write the result in — `gate_source` is known to each (cold start = `evaluate` + `regime == "cold_start"` → `"v1_cold_start"`).
  >   - **`training_cycle.py`** reads `promotion_decision["review_escalation"]["required"]` → `pause_flow_run` or straight to `register.py` act. The typed input's `RunInput.description` is populated from `reason` + `mode`, so the reviewer sees *"gate-boundary pass on pr_auc, 0.6 SE — your rejection overrides"* vs *"gate rejection — audit only, your verdict cannot promote."* **Not computed here** — an inline recompute is the "two independent evaluations of one rule become two rules" anti-pattern `CLAUDE.md` flags for the gate itself, and would strand the manual CLI / disaster-recovery path with no computed value.
  >   - **`register.py` act** replaces `if bool(cfg.register.require_review):` with `effective = decision["review_escalation"]["required"] or bool(cfg.register.require_review)`. One-directional: `cfg.register.require_review` can only **force review on** (spot-checking a routine clean pass, the DR path, the manual CLI's existing `require_review=true`), never suppress a computed `required: true`. An absent `review_escalation` field (artifacts predating this change) → fall back to the config bool alone.
  >   - **Supporting change — persist a real bootstrap `se` per criterion.** The boundary check is *"within `se_multiple` bootstrap SEs of the pass/fail line,"* so the SE has to be a real quantity, not an approximation. **Do not back it out of the persisted CI** (`se ≈ (ci_upper − ci_lower) / (2 × 1.959964)`): that is the normal approximation, and it diverges from the truth exactly where it matters — on a ~tens-of-churners reserve cohort the PR-AUC-delta bootstrap distribution is skewed (PR-AUC sits near the edge of its range), so percentile-CI-width and `1.96 × SE` disagree. Instead, **the bootstrap functions compute `std(ddof=1)` on their own resample array** before discarding it: `comparative_deltas` (per delta), `sealed_test_ranking_metrics` / `sealed_test_classification_report` / `sealed_test_calibration_report` (per absolute criterion — each already takes `n_bootstrap`/`random_state` and builds a resample distribution, so the SD is one call). The normal-approx fallback then only ever applies to a criterion that has a CI but no reachable resamples — none exist today. **Split of responsibility:** the SE is computed in those bootstrap helpers and persisted (full delta block → `metrics.json` per the reserve-metrics fix above; `se` / `delta_se` echoed into `promotion_decision.json["criteria"][<name>]`); the *margin* — `(value_or_delta − threshold, in the pass direction) / se` — is computed in `gate.py::decide_review_escalation`, because the 0.005 materiality bar, the `[0.80, 1.25]` band, and the pass direction are gate policy and stay in `gate.py`, the same separation `decide_promotion` already keeps. `build_gate_inputs` threads `se` into `GateInputs` (already interval-valued, one field per criterion). **Rare cycle:** same `comparative_deltas`, so `margin_in_se` is computed and persisted but **informational** — rare cycles are always-review regardless. **Cold start:** `se` persisted on the absolute criteria for consistency; no margin (always-review, no incumbent to be near a comparative boundary of). **Caveat to record:** at reserve-cohort *n* the bootstrap SE is itself a noisy estimate, so `se_multiple = 1.0` is a *soft* boundary, not a sharp line — acceptable because its only consequence is "route to a human," the fail-safe direction, but nobody should over-read `margin_in_se` 0.95 vs 1.05 as meaningful.
  >   - **Config.** `cfg.model_promotion.review_escalation.se_multiple` (default `1.0`) and `.criteria` (default `["pr_auc"]`, matching 10a-ii's "the deciding CI edge" wording; `["pr_auc", "recall", "brier", "calibration_slope"]` available for a stricter watch — a model clearing PR-AUC comfortably while its calibration-slope CI upper bound sits at 1.24 against a 1.25 bar is also a genuine close call). Both are visible strictness knobs, same pattern as `require_model_card_completeness`.
  >   - **Manual pre-Phase-10 flow unchanged:** `dvc repro` → `evaluate.py` writes `review_escalation` (cold start → `{required: true, mode: "veto"}`) → `models.review` stamps a verdict → `models.register` reads `required == true`, demands it. Identical behavior to today's explicit `require_review=true`, just computed rather than configured.
  >   - **Suspend only in `mode == "veto"` — audit-mode escalations do not block.** When `required` is set on a **gate rejection** (`mode == "audit"`), the version is already correctly rejected on the gate (`register.py:2205` tags `gate_fail` before review is even consulted), and the audit review's only product is a `promotion_review.json` note — *"a human looked at why it failed and recorded it"* (line 636: an audit-trail function, not a veto). So `training_cycle.py` does **not** `pause_flow_run` here: it runs `register.py` act immediately (→ `gate_fail`), then fires a **non-blocking** Prefect notification inviting a later manual `models.review` note. Nobody waits; nothing times out. `training_cycle.py` suspends **only** when `review_escalation.required and mode == "veto"` — a passing or boundary-passing gate whose human verdict could actually change the outcome.
  >   - **Review-window timeout → a sixth `_tag_rejected` reason, `"review_timed_out"`.** Because audit mode never suspends (above), a timeout can only occur on a **veto-mode** suspend — a passing gate awaiting a human — so the timed-out version always carries a non-None `promotion_decision.json` (unlike `pre_seal_screen_failed`), and `_rejection_description` needs no `None` special-case for it. **Mechanics:** **(1)** new `_rejection_description` branch that names `decision["review_escalation"]["reason"]` (e.g. *"gate-boundary pass on pr_auc, 0.6 SE"* / *"cold_start regime — mandatory"*) so a version-list scanner sees *why* review was pending and that it lapsed, plus *"re-trigger training_cycle.py to retry."* **(2)** new public `tag_review_timed_out(model_version, eval_run_id, cfg)` in `register.py`'s `__all__`, symmetric with `tag_pre_seal_screen_rejected`: resolves `client`/`registered_model_name`, loads `promotion_decision.json` off `eval_run_id`, calls `_tag_rejected(..., decision, "review_timed_out")` — `register.py` stays the sole registry-tag writer, and this inherits the same `__main__`-import allowance `tag_pre_seal_screen_rejected` already needs for `training_cycle.py` (if `pipelines/` grows more of these, extract `_tag_rejected` + the `tag_*` wrappers + `_rejection_description` into a plain `models/registry_tags.py`, the pattern `gate.py`/`artifacts.py`/`policy_config.py` already follow). **(3)** the `Suspend` carries a `timeout` comfortably under one training cycle (per line 660); its handler catches the Prefect timeout, calls `tag_review_timed_out`, and **returns normally** — flow ends `Completed`, not `Failed` (a recorded decision, not a crash), with no `register.py` act / no `shadow` staging / no `champion` flip. Retry is a fresh manual `training_cycle.py` trigger, which mints a *new* version; the timed-out one stays `rejected`. **Idempotency:** `_tag_rejected` re-setting the same tag/description is harmless on a handler retry (same property `_tag_gate_criteria` documents), and the timeout is authoritative once it fires — Prefect rejects a resume input submitted after the window closed.
  >   - **Resume-handler atomicity — the review stamp and the registry act must not replay as one unit.** The resume handler runs `run_review_step` (appends to `promotion_review.json["entries"]` and re-logs the *whole* doc via `log_dict`, which overwrites) then `run_registration_step`. If the second call fails and the handler retries, `run_review_step` re-runs first and appends a **duplicate identical entry** (same verdict/approver/notes, fresh `reviewed_at`) — one per retry. `gate.py::record_review` can't self-dedupe: its append-only contract is load-bearing for the genuine `rejected`→`approved` sequence (a `log_dict` overwrite would erase the first), so it can't tell "human submitted twice" from "orchestrator replayed once." Recoverable (`entries[-1]` is still right) but it pollutes the one document built to preserve review history. **Three-piece fix:** **(1)** split the resume handler into two Prefect `@task`s — `record_promotion_review` → `run_registration_act` — so a `run_registration_act` retry (the common case) never re-runs the review stamp. **(2)** same-triple dedupe guard in `run_review_step` for the flow-run-retry / manual-re-trigger edge Prefect *can* replay through: if the fetched `entries[-1]` already matches the incoming `(verdict, approver, notes)` (ignoring `reviewed_at`), skip the append + re-log, return the existing doc, log `review_entry_deduplicated` — ~4 lines, no schema/param change, only ever suppresses an immediately-consecutive identical re-stamp (a real reject→approve differs in verdict, a real re-review differs in notes), and also fixes an accidental manual `models.review` double-run. **(3)** a pre-existing `run_registration_step` re-entry bug this exposes: step 9 (`register.py:2156`) asserts `champion` still equals the decision's incumbent, so a retry that re-enters *after* a prior partial run flipped the alias (step 10) but *before* it tagged `promoted` (step 11) fails that assertion and wedges — alias pointing at an untagged version, `register.py` refusing to finish. Fix: step 9 tolerates *"incumbent matches **or** `champion` already points at `model_version`"*, the latter meaning "resume finalization (step 11), don't abort." **Not** a full idempotency key (Prefect flow-run id on the entry) — exact but more machinery than the failure warrants; the same-triple guard covers every realistic case.
  >   - **Cold-start `require_review` is satisfiable by an automated stamp today, and the shipped v1 proves it — make that structurally honest, not prose-only.** v1's `human_review` is `{review: "approved", approver: "Claude Code (automated local reinit)", notes: "...not a genuine model-quality review."}` — the mechanism refused to promote without a stamp (correct), but the *structured* fields read as "a reviewer approved this" and only the free-text `notes` disclaim it. That defeats the cold-start-specific rationale for `require_review` (no rollback target exists, so a human *must* look — and the card shows flagged equal-opportunity gaps up to 0.35), and it will recur on every Phase 12 fresh-environment cold start. **Fix (the honest version of what already happens, not a new bypass):** **(1)** add `review_kind: Literal["human", "automated_bootstrap"]` to the review entry — required; `gate.py::record_review` stamps it, `models/review.py` gets a `review.kind` param defaulting to `"human"`. **(2)** `"automated_bootstrap"` is accepted **only** paired with a reserved sentinel `review.approver="__automated_bootstrap__"` (enforced in `_require_approver`), so an automated stamp can never wear a name-shaped string. **(3)** it still satisfies `require_review` for every regime including cold start — blocking it just pushes a solo dev back to fake-human-name stamps; the field + the model-card callout (item (8) above) make the gap *visible* rather than *hidden*, which is the achievable honesty for a project with no real reviewers. **(4)** keeps the `review.py → record_review → promotion_review.json → register.py` path genuinely exercised end-to-end in the demo, which a plain bypass (`review_bypass_reason`) would not. **(5)** v1's card carries the new schema the next time `register.py` writes `model_card.json` for a champion (a one-off card-regen path, or at the next promotion); until then it's the acknowledged pre-fix artifact. Model-card task item (8) consumes `review_kind` for the `promotion_rationale` / `card_metadata.review_status` / Streamlit-callout reconciliation.
- `pipelines/drift_check.py` — daily 06:00; **reads the last 24 hours of rows from `prediction_log`** (`WHERE predicted_at > now() - interval '24 hours'`), not the API's structured JSON logs — see 10a-i's `prediction_log` bullet for why this flow and `performance_monitor.py` were unified onto one durable source rather than two different read paths for the same underlying prediction stream. Runs an Evidently data drift report **against `drift_reference.json` fetched from the currently-`champion`-aliased run** (Phase 7 `register.py`) — never against a fixed path or a re-derived baseline, so a rollback automatically restores the matching reference; alerts (Prefect notification) when PSI > 0.2 on any top-5 feature. **Prevalence is monitored alongside feature PSI and routes differently:** a sustained prevalence shift is a *re-calibration* trigger, not a retrain trigger (§9 limitation #12 — they are different remedies).
  - **✅ RESOLVED — this same check also closes the "does feature selection stay frozen forever" gap (below, in the frozen-decisions section), without waiting for Phase 13.** The retrain-trigger PSI check above is deliberately narrow (top-5 features only — the strongest, most stable ones, exactly the ones a feature-selection ablation is *least* likely to reconsider). Extend the PSI computation here to the **full `COMMITTED_FEATURES` set**, not just top-5, and route a breach on any feature *outside* the top-5 as a **distinct alert reason** — *"feature-selection review may be warranted"* — separate from the existing retrain-trigger notification. Same flow, same Evidently report, no new mechanism: just a wider watch list and a differently-labeled Prefect notification, so a human is pointed at the notebook review rather than a retrain. This also corrects the frozen-decisions bullet's framing below, which attributes the per-feature drift signal to "Phase 13's Evidently check" — Phase 13 builds the Grafana *dashboard* for this data, it doesn't compute the underlying PSI check for the first time; that computation already exists here, in 10b.
    **📍 Flagged for a real design pass when 10b work begins.** The direction is settled (extend the existing top-5 PSI check to the full `COMMITTED_FEATURES` set, route a non-top-5 breach as a distinct alert reason) but the concrete details aren't worked out yet: whether the same 0.2 PSI threshold applies outside the top-5 or a different one is warranted for weaker/borderline features, how this interacts with the existing daily prevalence check, the actual notification-channel/routing mechanics, and whether checking ~19 features instead of 5 meaningfully changes the daily Evidently run's cost. Resolve these alongside 10b's other open design questions, not silently by default when the code gets written.
  - **Same run also closes the shadow/canary named-consumer gap from Phase 9 — read-only, out-of-band, no new mechanism.** Phase 9's `shadow_canary_dual_score` log entries (`serving/predict.py::_score_batch`) have no consumer beyond the two live Prometheus metrics (`champion_challenger_score_distance`, `champion_challenger_agreement_total`) — nothing archives the per-request detail those aggregate over. `prediction_log`'s `dual_score_mode`/`challenger_probability`/`champion_probability` columns (populated on every shadow- or canary-evaluated row, per row — see 10a-i's `prediction_log` bullet) already carry this detail durably, so this run's existing 24h read additionally filters `WHERE dual_score_mode IS NOT NULL` and computes a windowed agreement-rate/score-distance summary, logged as a Prefect artifact (or a small JSON blob alongside the drift report) and alerted through the same Prefect-notification channel already wired for the PSI check above, on a sustained-disagreement threshold. **Why here and not a new flow:** the source, the schedule, and the read pattern (a bounded time-window query, not a live call against `predict.py`) are all already built for drift — a second flow duplicating that same read would be the "second mechanism for the same shape of problem" this project's own architecture notes elsewhere warn against. **Why Prefect and not the API itself:** `predict.py` is a synchronous, millisecond-budget request path — coupling it to Prefect's flow-run orchestration (task state tracking, typically a Prefect-server round-trip) for a purely diagnostic archival step would be the wrong tool in the request path, for no benefit the two live metrics don't already give a dashboard.
  - **⚠ Open detail: what makes a prevalence shift "sustained" is not yet specified.** Feature drift has a concrete number (PSI > 0.2); prevalence does not — the routing rule above names the concept but not the test. Prevalence is a single proportion, not a multi-bin distribution, so a PSI-style magnitude heuristic isn't the natural fit here the way it is for features; a two-proportion comparison (a confidence interval on the current-window churn rate against `drift_reference.json`'s stored prevalence, required to persist across multiple measurement windows rather than firing on one noisy reading) is a reasonable candidate when this module is actually built, but it is not yet a committed design.
  - **⚠ Open gap: no recalibration pipeline exists that is actually distinct from a full retrain.** The rule above says a prevalence shift should trigger recalibration rather than retraining, but nowhere in this plan is there a `recalibrate.py`-style flow analogous to `training_cycle.py` or the `rollback_champion()` helper — the only place `calibrate.py` runs at all is as one step inside `training_cycle.py`'s full cycle (`train → calibrate → evaluate → register`). As specified today, "recalibrate" and "retrain" resolve to the same pipeline, which defeats the reason for naming them separate remedies in the first place — recalibration is supposed to be the cheaper, faster fix, not a relabelled full cycle. Closing this gap needs a lightweight flow that reuses `calibrate.py` against the frozen, already-trained champion spec (no `train.py` refit, no feature or hyperparameter changes) and re-derives the threshold's contact-rate figure against the new calibration map — but that flow is not yet designed.
    **📍 Flagged for a real design pass when 10b work begins.** Surfaced in a 10a-ii walkthrough session discussing why industry MLOps practice generally treats retrain/recalibrate/rollback as three distinct remedies, not one collapsed "something's wrong, retrain" response: recalibration addresses **prior/label shift** (P(y) moving) specifically, which can leave ranking metrics (PR-AUC/ROC-AUC) fine while breaking probability calibration and anything downstream of it (`t* = c/(r×LTV)`, EV) — a real, statistically distinct failure mode from covariate drift or concept drift, not a lighter version of the same one (Saerens et al.'s prior-correction work is the canonical reference). The gap above is exactly what stands between that theory and this project actually having a working, cheaper-than-`training_cycle.py` remedy for it — resolve it as its own item before or alongside 10b's other design questions, not silently fold "recalibrate" into "retrain" by default. **Rollback, the third remedy named above, is not part of this gap — it already exists and is fully built** (`register.py::rollback_champion`, tag-based selection, current-champion exclusion, append-only `champion_history`). Of the three, only recalibrate is open.
- `pipelines/performance_monitor.py` — **realized-performance feedback loop (closes the ML loop — `input` drift is only a proxy for what we actually care about).** **Distinct from `pipelines/performance_check.py` (10a-ii)** — that module is the binding *promotion gate*, invoked once per training cycle to score a *candidate* against the reserve cohort and decide whether to promote it; this flow instead watches the already-*serving* champion continuously, on its own schedule, whether or not any training cycle is in progress. They share a naming root but not a job — the gate has a defendant (a candidate) and a verdict (promote/reject); this flow has neither, only an ongoing health reading. PSI and prediction-distribution drift detect that *inputs* moved; they do not measure whether the model is still *right*. For churn the ground truth does arrive — observed weeks/months later — so this flow joins **logged predictions → realised outcomes** once labels mature and computes **realised PR-AUC** (plus Brier and the decile-lift table) over the matured cohort — **both aggregate and sliced by the same Phase 7 robustness axes** (`contract_type`, `tenure_cohort`, `internetservice`), so segment-concentrated decay surfaces before it drags the aggregate down (the production analogue of Phase 7's V1 segment-collapse diagnostic — the continuous guard against subgroup collapse that a single sealed-test snapshot could never power) — comparing each to the Phase 7 offline estimate. **This is also where V2/V2b's enforcement actually lives**: the same matured cohorts are sliced by the **fairness axes** (`gender`, `seniorcitizen`, `has_partner`, `dependents`), computing realised equal-opportunity and demographic-parity gaps at the shipped `t*` (V2) and realised per-group calibration slope (V2b), each compared to its Phase 7 test-set baseline using the same segment-CI-derived alert-band approach as the robustness axes below — turning V1/V2/V2b from a one-time reported diagnostic (`ANALYSIS.md` §0) into a continuously-enforced one, once production volume supplies the power a single snapshot can't. **The baseline is Phase 7's *test-set* slice metrics, not its dev-OOF ones** — Phase 7 produces both, and they serve different purposes: the dev-OOF slices are non-gating diagnostics reported at promotion time (chosen for statistical power), while the test-set slices are the published metrics of record and what the model card told stakeholders. Production monitoring compares against what was published, so one number means one thing. **The thin-support caveat propagates:** a segment whose test baseline carries a wide CI (the two-year `contract_type` tier, ~10 churners) cannot support a tight degradation alert — derive that segment's alert band from its baseline CI rather than a fixed global threshold, or it will page on noise indefinitely. **Mechanics — reads `prediction_log` (10a-i's schema and write path), not the Phase 9 structured JSON log stream.** `prediction_outcomes` records the eventual churn label per `customerid`; the flow runs on a **label-maturity cadence** (e.g. monthly, after the observation window closes), scoring only the cohort whose outcomes are now known. **The join is `prediction_log ⋈ prediction_outcomes` on `customerid` plus a time condition** (most recent prediction before the outcome's observation date), not bare `customerid` — a customer can be scored more than once before their label matures, and a bare-`customerid` join risks one matured label fanning out to multiple prediction rows. **Delayed-label discipline:** never score a cohort before its outcome window matures (premature labels bias the metric optimistic); the maturity lag is a configured constant. **This flow is a second, independent consumer of the same two tables `training_pool`'s reshape already writes from — not the retraining feedstock itself.** `training_pool` **is** the union this bullet used to describe as a live query — the union just moved from read-time to write-time. 10a-ii built two append-only write paths into one materialized table: `ingest.py`'s one-time seed (`customers_raw`, `reserve_month IS NULL`), and `training_cycle.py` (10b)'s cyclical reshape function (`data/training_pool.py`), which joins `prediction_log ⋈ prediction_outcomes` per matured reserve cohort and **appends** the flattened result with that cohort's `reserve_month` set — never a delete-and-reload, so each cycle's contribution lands once and stays. `sql/features/*.sql`/`features/build.py` already read from `training_pool` directly (10a-ii repointed `FROM customers_raw` → `FROM training_pool`) with no join or union logic of their own, because the union was already resolved by the two write paths that put the rows there in the first place. **populated for real, but only for the two cycles the `reserve` partition (10a-ii) supports**: the reserve carve-out feeds genuine customers through the real serving path into `prediction_log`, and `outcomes.py`'s `source="synthetic_seed"` path matures them into `prediction_outcomes` on the manufactured release schedule, which is exactly the mechanism that supersedes `ANALYSIS.md` §9 #13 for those two cycles (see the reserve-partition resolution above). Beyond the reserve's two-cycle scope — or absent that mechanism entirely — `training_pool` gets no further feed past its one-time seed, since nothing about this project's static dataset creates outcomes past what the reserve manufactures. **The JSON log stream (Phase 9, → CloudWatch in Phase 12) plays no role in this join or in retraining generally, full stop** — it remains a separate, ops-only consumer of the same underlying prediction computation, easy to mistake for a second copy of this durable record but never one: see the runbook's incident-triage use (Phase 14, pulling a specific `request_id`/`customerid` for forensic debugging) and the CloudWatch-consumer discussion (Phase 12) for its actual, ongoing job. This realised number — not PSI — is the **authoritative health signal** and the primary, *performance-based* retrain trigger (see below).
  > **📌 Champion-vs-challenger comparison metrics — mostly already provisioned; two gaps to close, plus a shared slicing core `performance_monitor.py` owns.** The Phase 9 serving metrics (`champion_challenger_agreement_total` = live decision-disagreement, `champion_challenger_score_distance`), `drift_check.py`'s `dual_score_mode IS NOT NULL` windowed rollup (line 729), and `nightly_batch_predict.py`'s decision-set parity (line 742) already cover most of this. `performance_monitor.py` watches a serving champion with *no challenger*, so disagreement/overlap metrics are not its steady-state job — they live in the two-model contexts. What `performance_monitor.py` contributes is its **sliced-metric implementation, extracted as a reusable function**: realized PR-AUC / Brier / decile-lift / equal-opportunity / demographic-parity / per-group calibration slope, each sliced by the Phase 7 robustness axes (`contract_type`, `tenure_cohort`, `internetservice`) and fairness axes (`gender`, `seniorcitizen`, `has_partner`, `dependents`) with CI-derived per-segment alert bands. Three callers share it:
  >   - **📌 Flagged 2026-10-19, not yet resolved: demographic parity doesn't need the label-maturity wait the other five sliced metrics do.** Of the six metrics this shared core computes, five (realized PR-AUC, Brier, decile-lift, equal opportunity, per-group calibration slope) all need `churn_realized`, so gating them behind `prediction_log ⋈ prediction_outcomes` is correct. **Demographic parity is the odd one out** — it's a rate over *predictions* (`decision`/`contact` by fairness-axis group), computable straight off `prediction_log` with no outcomes join, the same no-label immediacy Tier 1 of the shadow-analysis join below already exploits for score distributions and disagreement rate. Bundling it into the label-gated job isn't wrong, just a missed cadence: it could run on the same fast schedule as `drift_check.py` instead of waiting a full label-maturity cycle to catch a contact-rate disparity that was already visible the day the predictions were made. Worth splitting out when this sliced-metric core is actually implemented — mechanism/scheduling TBD at that point.
  >   - **The shadow-analysis join, concretely — one durable source, two tiers.** **Tier 1, `prediction_log` alone (no join):** every shadow row already carries `champion_probability`, `challenger_probability`, `dual_score_mode='shadow'`, `threshold`, `customerid`, `feature_snapshot`, `route` — so score distributions, disagreement rate, score distance, and segment concentration all compute from those columns directly, no labels, available immediately. **Tier 2, `prediction_log ⋈ prediction_outcomes` (label-dependent — realized PR-AUC / calibration / recall / Brier):** join on `customerid` **plus the time condition** (most recent prediction before the outcome's `observed_at` — never a bare `customerid` join, per the fan-out rule in `performance_monitor.py`'s own bullet), target column **`churn_baseline`** (the untreated label — the `outcomes.py` split routes offline/analysis surfaces to baseline). Champion metrics on `champion_probability`, challenger on `challenger_probability`, same rows, same labels — a clean **paired** comparison, because a shadow challenger never served, so there is no served-outcome confound (canary rows differ — the challenger served the 5%, which is the slice-vs-slice path below). **Caveats:** `contact` is populated for `batch`/`nightly_batch` routes only (null for `route='single'`), so contact-level analysis is batch-only; rows where the challenger errored or returned NaN are simply absent from both tiers (Gap B's counter is the only way to see the hole); on this static dataset shadow rows exist only from reserve-cohort seeding + `nightly_batch_predict.py` runs while `shadow.enabled`. This is the standard industry shape (durable inference log with dual scores, delayed-label join on entity + time window, feature vector logged as-served, paired offline metrics) — a larger shop swaps Postgres for a warehouse, the ad-hoc query for a scheduled dbt/Spark job, and the hand-rolled rollup for a bought tool (Evidently / Arize / SageMaker Model Monitor), none of which change the recipe.
  >   - **`performance_monitor.py` itself** — serving champion, `churn_realized` labels, its existing job.
  >   - **The `require_traffic_validation` canary window** — canary slice vs champion control slice on `churn_realized` (the `outcomes.py` treatment-effect label): the genuine slice-vs-slice A/B. Low power (a few hundred canary customers), reported with CIs, never gating.
  >   - **`drift_check.py`'s dual-score rollup** — breaks the decision-disagreement rate down by the same axes. **Gap 1 — segment concentration:** an 8% overall disagreement that is 30% within `contract_type = month-to-month` is a targeting shift a reviewer must see before promotion, and no aggregate metric surfaces it; nothing computes this today.
  >   - **Gap 2 — contact-set overlap.** `nightly_batch_predict.py` (line 742) computes decision-set Jaccard at `decision` level (`proba ≥ t*`) but calls it "the actual contact list" — it is not. `contact` is `decision` after the `min(contact_capacity, ⌊campaign_budget / cost⌋)` EV-ranked cut, a batch operation. Add a **`contact`-level Jaccard** alongside the `decision`-level one; the retention team works off `contact`, so that is the operationally meaningful overlap and the one whose swing actually costs budget.
  >   - **Business KPIs — two distinct readings, do not conflate.** **(a) `performance_monitor.py`'s champion loop** — realized EV / retained revenue / campaign cost / contacted count / break-even / saved-per-contact on each matured *champion* cohort (the bulk of traffic — 95% when canary is on, 100% when off; **no canary dependency**), via `economics.py` (dataset-agnostic — same reuse `performance_check.py` does), `costs_config_hash`-tagged so a shift is attributable to the model vs. `costs.yaml`, plotted across cohorts. This is the deployed policy's *absolute* realized outcome; a downward trend is a genuine standalone decay signal (it closes the ML loop). The `outcomes.py` treatment effect is what makes retained-revenue / saved-per-contact real rather than noise. **(b) The canary slice-vs-slice Δ** (challenger vs champion-control, needs the 5%, read in the `require_traffic_validation` window) — under flat treatment efficacy this Δ *is* a monetized restatement of the contact-set precision difference, i.e. the predictive metric + sampling noise; kept for the stakeholder dashboard and the model card's realized-vs-projected line, not independent evidence. Neither ever gates (§0: EV is always diagnostic).
  >   - **Operational KPIs — two Phase 9 `serving/predict.py` instrumentation gaps, surfaced here, land with the 10b PR.** What's provisioned: `predictions_total{scoring_path, model_version}` (volume, scoring-path-labelled), `predicted_probability{model_version}`, `predictions_above_threshold_total{model_version}`, `champion_challenger_score_distance{mode}`, `champion_challenger_agreement_total{outcome, mode}`, and the challenger's full per-row score in `prediction_log` (`challenger_probability`, joinable to `prediction_outcomes`).
  >     - **Gap A — no per-model latency split.** The only latency metric is whole-request HTTP latency from `prometheus_fastapi_instrumentator` (`serving/app.py`); the challenger is scored *inline* within the champion's request (`_score_batch`), so its scoring time is buried inside that one number — a challenger 5× slower than the champion is invisible. Add a `challenger_score_seconds{mode}` histogram around the inline challenger `predict_proba` call.
  >     - **Gap B — no challenger error / NaN counter.** The challenger's inline failures are wrapped in `try/except` **on purpose** — a challenger bug must never break the customer's response, which the champion already produced — so an exception or a NaN posterior is caught, logged, and the request still returns HTTP 200. Every existing metric then reads healthy (no 5xx for the instrumentator to count) while the challenger — the model being evaluated for promotion — is silently failing on some or all requests; those rows drop *out* of the shadow/canary analysis set (biasing it toward the subset that didn't fail), and a challenger that can't score in production looks promotable. Add `challenger_error_total{mode, kind}` (`kind` = `exception` / `nan_output` / `timeout` / `schema_mismatch`), incremented in the existing `except` plus a finite-check on the challenger output. A sustained challenger error rate above a small threshold is then a **hard block on promotion**, routed into the `canary_analysis` verdict / `require_traffic_validation` gate.
  > **📌 Retraining is trigger-based, not only scheduled.** The monthly cron is the *floor*; a sustained realised-PR-AUC drop below a configured fraction of the Phase 7 baseline (or a sustained PSI breach) should *trigger* `training_cycle.py` off-cycle. Schedule-only retraining either wastes compute when nothing moved or reacts too slowly when something did. Connect `performance_monitor.py` / `drift_check.py` alerts to an event-triggered run of the retrain flow, with the monthly schedule as the backstop.
  > **📌 Two breach→response edges, not one — a fresh promotion and a stale champion need different remedies.** The trigger above wires drift/performance breaches to `training_cycle.py`; that is the correct *steady-state* remedy (the world moved, learn from it), but it is the wrong tool in the window right after a `champion` flip. There the previous champion is still in the registry tagged `promotion_status: promoted`, known-good, and one `rollback_champion()` away (Phase 7 `register.py`) — so the remedy for *"the model we promoted an hour ago is misbehaving"* is **revert to the incumbent**, not retrain, which takes a full cycle and, if the promotion itself was the fault (a silently-failed calibration, a data-quality regression), may just reproduce it. So promotion **opens a monitored bake-in window** (a configured N batches / T hours, keyed off `register.py`'s `promoted_at` marker) rather than ending at the smoke check: a breach *inside* the window routes to `rollback_champion()`; a breach *outside* it routes to the retrain trigger above. This closes the lifecycle loop — **promote → watch → (trust | revert)** — and reuses the rollback helper Phase 7 already builds rather than inventing a second revert path. **⚠ On this static dataset the window's live watch is a legible stub, not fabricated** — same discipline as `require_traffic_validation`: the routing distinction and the rollback path are real and testable; the anomaly-triggered auto-revert on live traffic is explicitly *not* wired to fake data (§9 #13). Auto-reverting on a monitoring blip also risks promote↔rollback flapping, which is why even in production this edge is gated rather than fully automatic — the demonstrable value recorded here is the *design*: that the two remedies are distinct and which one the post-promotion window selects.
- **Log destination for the pipeline stages — `LOG_FORMAT=json` on the Prefect worker, and Prefect flow-run logs as the system of record.** Every stage this flow triggers emits structlog events (`validation_error`, `merge_complete`, `model_promoted`, the dummy-floor and `within_ci` alarms above), and until this phase they go to whatever terminal ran `dvc repro`. Once the flow is unattended there is no terminal, so the destination has to be chosen rather than inherited: set `LOG_FORMAT=json` in the worker's environment so `utils/logging.py:58` selects the JSON renderer instead of the console one, and let Prefect capture stdout per flow run. This is what makes the Phase 8 validation-receipt routing complete — the receipt answers *what* the gates decided, the flow-run log answers *when it ran and what else happened around it*, and neither is asked to do the other's job. Note the retention difference from Phase 12's CloudWatch: Prefect's log retention is bounded by its own database, so treat it as an operational record, not an audit trail.

- **The Prefect worker container needs no volume mount for `reports/` — the receipt files it depends on (`reports/train_receipt.json`, `reports/calibrate_receipt.json`; Phase 8's B0 prerequisite) are ephemeral per-cycle scratch state, not durable pipeline state.** `training_cycle.py`'s full `ingest → ... → register` chain runs inside one worker container invocation, so each stage reads the previous stage's receipt straight off that same container's own (ephemeral) filesystem before the container exits — no different from a local run, and no different in principle from this section's `POSTGRES_URL`-for-Optuna-storage note above: don't reach for a mount to make a locally-scoped file durable, wire the actual durable services (`MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI`, `POSTGRES_URL`) explicitly instead, and let anything genuinely ephemeral live and die with the container that produced it. The one legitimate reason to touch `reports/` from outside the container is a human wanting to inspect it after a failed run — Phase 11's `data-quality.yml` `upload-artifact` step is the model for that (a one-shot copy-out for debugging), not a persistent volume the pipeline itself depends on. This generalizes cleanly to Phase 11's CI job too: `dvc repro` there also runs the whole DAG inside one job/container, so the same reasoning is why that job needs no `reports/` mount either.
  > **✅ RESOLVED — landed ahead of this phase rather than deferred to it: all six receipts (`ingest`/`validation`/`train`/`calibrate`/`eval`/`error_analysis`) moved from git-committed to gitignored.** Raised during Phase 8's fresh-clone verification as an open question with two candidates (see the git history of this bullet for the original writeup and trade-off analysis). Candidate 1 — gitignore the six receipts, matching every other `reports/*.json` MLflow mirror — was chosen over candidate 2 (a `find_dangling_receipts` pre-flight check in `training_cycle.py`): it makes this bullet's "ephemeral per-cycle scratch state, not durable pipeline state" framing true of the code, not just the prose, and lets DVC's own missing-output check catch a fresh Postgres/MLflow pair automatically — the same way it already does for `split`/`features` — rather than requiring an explicit guard to be wired into every place a receipt gets trusted, including this phase's `training_cycle.py`. `CLAUDE.md`'s Data Handling section carries the full rationale; `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s and `README.md`'s now-unnecessary manual `--force` workarounds were removed in the same pass. The adjacent CI-sketch question (what makes the DAG rerun vs. skip a stage, for a scheduled run vs. a manual one) is unaffected by this and remains open above.

- `pipelines/nightly_batch_predict.py` (optional) — **named to avoid exactly the confusion its old name (`batch_predict.py`) invited: this is not `/predict/batch`.** `/predict/batch` (`serving/app.py`, Phase 9) is a synchronous FastAPI endpoint — a caller supplies a list of customers, gets scores back in the response, and Streamlit's Batch tab already exercises it. This module is a scheduled, unattended Prefect flow with no caller at all: it sweeps the *entire* customer population nightly, on its own, and its actual job (see the `require_traffic_validation` note below) is comparing the champion against a staged shadow candidate, not serving anyone. Writes results into **`prediction_log`** (`route="nightly_batch"` — distinct from `/predict/batch`'s own `route="batch"` rows, never conflated with them), not a separate `predictions` table — `prediction_log`'s schema (10a-i) already handles a nightly sweep generically, as just another row shape. One table, one schema, rather than two near-identical tables with an unclear boundary between them.
  > **📌 This is the vehicle that makes the `require_traffic_validation` seam (above) actually runnable — batch shadow scoring.** The traffic-validation gate between the promotion decision and the `champion` flip is a no-op stub without something to run in it; batch scoring is where it runs, because scoring a whole cohort offline lets you compare two models directly with zero traffic-splitting infrastructure. Concretely: score each batch with **both** the incumbent `champion` **and** the staged candidate (resolved via the `shadow` alias the parameterized `promote_to_alias` from Phase 7 `register.py` sets). **Only `champion`'s outputs drive real actions; the candidate's are recorded, never acted on** — this is shadow mode, so the blast radius is exactly zero. Compare the two on **score-distribution PSI** (built on the same quantile-binning machinery `models/drift_reference.py` already uses for the champion's score baseline — **note:** `_quantile_bin_edges` is private and not in that module's `__all__` today, so this reuse needs it exported, or the ~8 lines duplicated, when this module is actually written) and **decision-set parity** — the Jaccard overlap of who crosses `t*` (`decision` level), computed via `economics.py` and the shipped threshold, **plus a second Jaccard at `contact` level** (after the `min(contact_capacity, ⌊campaign_budget / cost⌋)` EV-ranked cut — the list the retention team actually works from; `decision` and `contact` are different sets and only the latter's swing costs budget). A large unexpected swing in *who gets contacted* is the batch analog of a canary going red, and it is the failure that costs campaign budget — which no offline gate or latency check would surface. **No labels are needed**, so unlike the realised-performance loop this runs immediately.
  > - **⚠ Same honesty discipline as the retrain gate — this is a mechanism demonstration, not live traffic.** The dataset is the static 7,043-row snapshot with no live feed (`CLAUDE.md`; the open-question notes in 10a), so "each batch" is the same frozen rows every cycle. The shadow *plumbing* — dual scoring, PSI, decision-set diff, alerting — is real and correct; the *statistical claim* that it is watching production traffic is not. Structure it so the comparison is genuine and the absence of a live feed is legible, exactly as this phase's `training_cycle.py` bullet already requires of the `require_traffic_validation` gate itself (the "two calls to `register.py`'s act step" note above): **do not fabricate traffic to make the shadow diff look live.** Uplift measurement (A/B on realised churn) stays out of scope — it needs maturing labels this dataset never produces (§9 #13) and is noted as a next step only.
- `scripts/seed_reserve_cohort.py` — **a thin demo/seeding driver, not a new production write path.** Given a `reserve_month`, it reads `reserve_ids()`, filters to that month's customerids, and drives two *already-built* write paths in sequence rather than owning any persistence logic of its own: **(1)** POSTs those customerids to `/predict/batch` — `prediction_log`'s `BackgroundTasks` write (10a-i) fires automatically, no new code needed; **(2)** calls `serving/outcomes.py`'s write path for the same cohort, `source="synthetic_seed"`, maturing their labels into `prediction_outcomes`. This is the piece `run_performance_check` (`performance_check.py`) and `training_pool`'s write-path-2 reshape (`data/training_pool.py`, "called by `training_cycle.py` once per matured reserve cohort") both depend on existing *before* a routine cycle can run — without it, `_load_comparison_cohort`/`_score_candidate_on_reserve` have nothing to read and raise. **Lives in `scripts/`, not `src/telco_churn/`** — like `scripts/generate_sample_batch_predictions.py`, it orchestrates existing production write paths for demonstration purposes rather than being one itself, so it carries none of the `__main__`-CLI subprocess-test obligations a real `telco_churn` module would. **`observed_at` gets a realistic offset, not `now()`, even though both steps run back-to-back in real time** — `outcomes.py`'s schema already separates `observed_at` ("the real-world date the outcome became known") from `recorded_at` ("when this row was written") specifically so a script like this can stamp a semantically honest maturity lag (e.g. `predicted_at` + a configured number of weeks) without needing to actually wait that long — preserving the "labels take real time to mature" truth this project states elsewhere (`training_cycle.py`'s monthly-cadence rationale above) even though the seeding itself is instant.
- `scripts/run_demo_cycle.py` — **a demo-orchestration convenience wrapper, kept deliberately outside `training_cycle.py` itself.** Sequences this project's entire fixed, 2-cycle demo in one command: `seed_reserve_cohort(1) → seed_reserve_cohort(2) → trigger training_cycle.py(evaluation_month=2) → seed_reserve_cohort(3) → trigger training_cycle.py(evaluation_month=3)`. **Why this doesn't live inside `training_cycle.py`:** `scripts/seed_reserve_cohort.py` is explicitly demo-only scaffolding (its own bullet above) standing in for real customer traffic this static dataset can never generate — a real deployment's `training_cycle.py` would never call it, since real `prediction_log`/`prediction_outcomes` rows would already exist from real API calls. Calling it from inside `training_cycle.py` would make the flow that's supposed to model real production orchestration silently dependent on demo-only synthetic-data code — the same boundary the "do not fabricate traffic to make the gate appear to run" rule (above) already protects for the shadow/canary window. Keeping this sequencing in its own `scripts/`-only driver gets the one-command convenience without blurring that line. **Which months get seeded, in what order, is still explicit, not auto-detected** — same reasoning as `evaluation_month`'s own flow-parameter design above: a fixed, already-known 2-cycle demo doesn't need "figure out what's next" logic, just a literal, readable sequence. Lives in `scripts/`, not `src/telco_churn/`, for the same reason `seed_reserve_cohort.py` does — it orchestrates already-built pieces for demonstration purposes rather than being a production write path of its own.

**Verification:** Trigger each flow from the Prefect UI; inject synthetic rows with shifted `MonthlyCharges`; confirm the drift alert fires within the next scheduled drift check run. **Rollback-fidelity check (the reason the reference is version-scoped):** with two promoted versions, re-point `champion` at the older one and confirm `drift_check.py` now measures against *that* version's `drift_reference.json` and its alert state returns to baseline — a run that still reports drift after a rollback is reading a fixed path, which is the bug this design exists to prevent. `grep -rn "reports/drift_reference" pipelines/ src/telco_churn/` returns nothing: no code reads the mirrored copy, it is for humans only. Run `scripts/seed_reserve_cohort.py` for a reserve month and confirm `performance_monitor.py` computes a realised PR-AUC, compares it to the Phase 7 baseline, and (when forced below the configured fraction) fires the performance-based retrain trigger.

#### Phase 10c — Progressive Deployment *(3–4 days)*

**Added 2026-08-29 after a walkthrough of how registration and rollout are actually staged in industry ML systems.** 10b already specifies the pieces this package builds as *pluggable seams* — `require_traffic_validation` (default off), the `challenger`-alias shadow/canary surface, `performance_monitor.py`'s extracted sliced-metric core, `outcomes.py`'s retention treatment effect and `churn_baseline`/`churn_realized` split, and the single `alias=champion` act call. What 10b does **not** have is the industry-standard *separation* of "cleared to deploy" from "serving," a progressive canary ramp with guardrail-gated advance and auto-rollback, a continuous traffic source to drive that ramp, and — optionally — a compressed label clock that lets the canary window produce a real online verdict. 10c adds exactly those, on top of a finished 10b. **Purely additive: 10a and 10b ship and run without it**, and cold start is untouched (see the v1 note below).

**What this achieves:** the registration → deployment path built to the shape a mature MLOps org runs — a four-state registry lifecycle where the offline gate + review produces an `approved` artifact that is *not yet serving*, a separate progressive-delivery step ramps a canary and either promotes or auto-rolls-back on guardrails, and a scripted-but-continuous traffic feed makes the shadow/canary metrics move. With the compressed-clock mode on, the same machinery also demonstrates the *fast-label* regime (online experiment decides, offline is a pre-filter) alongside this project's native *slow-label* regime (offline backtest decides, online confirms safety) — both industry patterns in one codebase. **Depends on 10a-i, 10a-ii, and 10b being done.**

**Deliverables:**

- **Registry lifecycle split — `pending → approved → staged → promoted`.** The `promotion_status` tag vocabulary extends from `pending | promoted | rejected` to `pending | approved | staged | promoted | rejected`. `run_registration_step` splits into two entry points: **`approve_challenger`** — gate verdict + review pass → tag `approved`, **stop; no alias flip** — this is SageMaker's `Approved` state, "cleared to deploy," and is where a routine cycle's act step now ends when `require_traffic_validation` is on; and **`promote_approved`** — invoked by `canary_rollout.py` only after a clean ramp → load by explicit version → full smoke check (schema/range, golden parity, environment parity, latency) → tag `promoted` → the one `alias=champion` act call → re-assert parity through the alias → roll the alias back on failure. `staged` is a tag write by `canary_rollout.py` at the moment it starts routing canary traffic, not a `register.py` act. `_tag_rejected` / `_rejection_description` gain an eighth `reason`, `"canary_guardrail_breach"` (10b's review-timeout and pre-seal bullets take the count to seven; this is the eighth — update the `_rejection_description` docstring count and the `raise ValueError` fallback stays the loud backstop).
  > **📌 "One registration per training cycle" and "one act call, `alias=champion`" both still hold.** `register_challenger` still mints exactly once, at the top of the cycle (10b, line 657). `approve_challenger` writes only a tag — it flips nothing. The single `champion` flip is still the single act call, just relocated from "end of `run_registration_step`" to "`promote_approved`, after the ramp." `staged` is a status tag, not an alias and not an act. So `CLAUDE.md`'s registry invariants are unchanged in substance; only the lifecycle the tag records gets one more legitimate resting state on each side of `promoted`.
  > **📌 `approved` and `staged` are explicitly *not* rollback targets — the rollback query is untouched.** `rollback_champion` still selects the highest version tagged `promotion_status: promoted`, and `approved`/`staged` are deliberately outside that set: a candidate mid-ramp is being *tested*, not validated-and-parked. The stale-`pending` reaper (`CLAUDE.md` Phase 14) gains a carve-out for `approved` and `staged` symmetric to 10b's existing "never reap the version holding `@challenger`" rule — belt-and-braces, since the candidate keeps `@challenger` through the whole window anyway (10b, line 657), but it makes the intent legible by status as well as by alias.
  > **📌 v1 / cold start: `pending → approved → promoted`, `staged` skipped.** Cold start has `require_traffic_validation` forced off (10b, line 665 — no serving incumbent, no traffic baseline, nothing for a slice-vs-slice guardrail to compare against), so `canary_rollout.py` never runs and `staged` is never set. `approve_challenger` tags `approved` after the mandatory cold-start review, then `promote_approved` runs immediately and *creates* (not flips) `@champion`. Compressed-clock mode is irrelevant to v1 — the online-decides path needs a champion-vs-challenger A/B, and v1 is the first model. This is the same path 10b already describes for cold start, with one extra tag state passed through.

- **`rollback_champion` hardening — bring the recovery path up to the forward path's bar.** Cross-checked `register.py::rollback_champion` (`register.py:440`) against `_flip_and_confirm` (`register.py:1904`) and industry revert practice (Argo Rollouts / Flagger / SageMaker deployment rollback all re-run the health check on the reverted version). The core discipline is sound — tag-based selection (never `N-1`), explicit target still validated for `promoted`, current-champion exclusion, append-only `promotion_log`, unset-vs-raise when no known-good exists — but four gaps, all on the *recovery* path, which today lacks protection the *forward* path has:
  - **(A) No post-rollback verification.** `_flip_and_confirm` does flip → `load_model("models:/name@alias")` → `_check_golden_parity` → revert on failure. `rollback_champion` just flips and returns, so a rollback to a version whose serialized artifact is corrupt or unloadable reports success with `@champion` pointing at a broken model. **Fix:** after the flip, load through the alias and re-check golden parity (reuse `_flip_and_confirm`'s body, or extract the shared "flip + confirm" core). If the rollback *target* also fails, fall through to the existing unset-and-return-`None` terminal state and log at `error` — there is no known-good state, and a human must be paged.
  - **(B) No environment-parity check on the target.** The forward path runs `check_environment_parity` on the challenger before promoting; rollback flips blind.
